- [NVIDIA Local AI blog series, Aug 2026](sources/nvidia-local-ai-blog-series-2026-08.md) — rolling vendor roundup (Aug 11–25). **[Nemotron 3.5 Lightning](entities/nemotron.md)** (30B MoE, "4× faster tokens" with no named baseline), **[NeMo Switchyard](entities/nemo-switchyard.md)** (per-step model routing, claimed **one-third the cost of Opus 4.8** on unnamed internal benchmarks), Qwen3.8-27B at **131 tok/s on an RTX 5090** (Q4_K_M + speculative decoding — not a raw figure), Meta Muse Glimmer, and a model-fits-box ladder from [Cosmos 3 Edge](sources/nvidia-cosmos3-edge-hf-blog.md) 4B up to 284B on a DGX Station. **Jetson is named on nearly every item, but every named application is a desktop one.** (2026-08-11)
- [Introducing Portable Computer for local-first AI (Perplexity)](sources/perplexity-portable-computer.md) — recovered via **Wayback**, since perplexity.ai 403s every fetch path. The announcement NVIDIA paraphrased — and NVIDIA dropped the **Pro/Max subscription gate**, **Linux-only** launch, **per-transfer user consent**, **fail-closed sandboxing**, and the fact that the **orchestrator, planner, tool router, scheduler, durable task queue and search index all run on device**. (2026-08-25)
- [A Local-First Agent for Private and Cost-Effective Knowledge Work (Perplexity Research)](sources/perplexity-local-first-agent-research.md) — **the substantive one.** Co-designing a harness and a model for local inference, with numbers. Same model, same box, three harnesses: **65.1% / 34.6% / 13.9%** on multimodal documents, and the worst spent **41× more tokens** — the harness was the whole difference. Reports that Qwen 3.8 27B advertises **260K context but struggles past 100K**. Prices advisor escalation: local 59.6% → **73.0%** at $0.415/rollout vs **82.4%** frontier-only at $0.65. Vendor-run throughout, and **BrowseComp is confounded** — Computer used Perplexity's own search engine while the baselines used Brave. (2026-08-25)

### Figure 03 — the platform, the model, and the factory (ingested 2026-08-28)
Six Figure primaries. The pattern across them: **hardware and manufacturing disclosures are specific and checkable; the AI disclosures contain no success rate, no baseline, no benchmark.**
- [Introducing Figure 03](sources/figure-03-announcement.md) — the 2025-10-09 announcement. The generational change is **sensing and unit cost**, not strength: cameras at **2× frame rate / ¼ latency / 60% wider FOV**, a **palm camera per hand** for when the head camera is occluded, in-house **fingertip tactile at 3 g** ("the weight of a paperclip"), **10 Gbps mmWave** fleet offload, **2 kW inductive charging through coils in the feet**, multi-density foam and washable soft textiles, **−9% mass** vs Figure 02, and a shift from **CNC machining to die-casting/injection moulding/stamping** for [BotQ](entities/botq.md). Contains **no height, weight, DOF, payload, runtime, price or compute** — the numbers everyone quotes from it are not in it. (2025-10-09)
- [Figure 03 product page](sources/figure-03-product-page.md) — Figure's only official spec table, filed separately because the announcement has none: **5'8" (~173 cm) · 61 kg · 20 kg payload · 5 h · 1.2 m/s · electric**. Settles three numbers the secondary ecosystem gets wrong — **168 cm** (that was Figure 02's), **40 DOF** (in no Figure primary), **4-hour runtime** (both primaries say 5 h) — and exposes the wiki's own error: Figure 02 was **~67 kg**, not ~60. Also claims household tasks "all autonomously" in the present tense for a robot that is not for sale. (retrieved 2026-08-28)
- [F.03 Battery Development](sources/figure-f03-battery.md) — the most technically substantive Figure primary in the wiki. **2.3 kWh → 5 h**, 2 kW fast charge, **94% energy-density gain since F.01**, **−78% cost vs F.02**, a **structural pack** that is a load-bearing torso member and survives a **1 m drop onto concrete from any orientation**, and four-layer safety down to a **wirebond interconnect deliberately tuned as a fuse**. UN38.3 certified; first humanoid battery in process for **UL 2271** — a standard Figure helped an **OSHA NRTL create because none existed**. Implies a **~460 W whole-robot power budget** that must also cover onboard Helix inference. (2025-07-17)
- [Introducing Helix 02 — Full-Body Autonomy](sources/figure-helix-02.md) — the wiki's only **three-tier** VLA. New **System 0**: 10M params at **1 kHz**, trained on **1,000+ h of retargeted human motion** across **200k parallel sim envs**, which Figure says **"replaces 109,504 lines of hand-engineered C++."** S1 is rewired to **"all sensors in, all actuators out"** — head + palm cameras, fingertip tactile, proprioception → legs, torso, arms, fingers — and *requires* Figure 03 hardware. Demo: a **4-minute, 61-action** autonomous dishwasher task, closing a drawer with its hip and lifting the door with its foot. **No success rate, no baseline, no benchmark**; "longest horizon task by a humanoid to date" is unfalsifiable as stated. (2026-01-27)
- [Ramping Figure 03 Production](sources/figure-ramping-03-production.md) — the numbers that behave like manufacturing numbers. **350+ units delivered**; rate **1/day → 1/hour (24× in under 120 days)**; **80%+ end-of-line first-pass yield** volunteered unprompted; **99.3%** on the battery line; **9,000+ actuators across 10+ SKUs**; 80+ functional tests and thousands-of-cycle burn-in per robot. Plus fleet infrastructure — OTA, Field Service Management, **formal recall campaigns**, software **"fallback ladders"** — and **perception-conditioned System 0**: head-camera RGB lifted to 3D, RL over thousands of randomised terrains, **zero-shot to real stairs**. (2026-04-29)
- [F.03 Arrives at BMW](sources/figure-03-at-bmw.md) — Figure 03 at BMW Spartanburg Hall 52, succeeding Figure 02's **30,000 cars**. Task moves from pick-and-place to **sequencing**: parts that "do not arrive in mathematically perfect orientations," needing perception-driven correction *plus* mobility *plus* two force regimes (thin-walled parts and pulling a caster-wheeled cart). The clearest humanoid-form argument in the wiki — "structurally infeasible… with traditional, fixed automation or six-axis robotic arm" — and **no throughput, cycle time, success rate or unit count**. (2026-06-30)

### Project Go-Big — how Figure crosses the human→robot gap (ingested 2026-08-28)
The follow-up to the Figure 03 ingest, chasing the one question [Index](entities/figure-index.md) refuses to answer. Figure *has* an answer — and it covers the easy half.
- [Project Go-Big — Internet-Scale Humanoid Pretraining and Direct Human-to-Robot Transfer](sources/figure-project-go-big.md) — [Helix](entities/helix.md) trained on **100% egocentric human video**, collected in [Brookfield](entities/brookfield.md) homes, performing closed-loop navigation from commands like *"go to the fridge"* with **"no robot demonstrations whatsoever."** One network now emits both manipulation and navigation. **The claim is carefully scoped**: *"low-level **SE(2)** velocity commands"* — planar position and heading, the one case where the morphology gap essentially vanishes (a person and a humanoid walking to the same fridge trace nearly the same path) and where the action labels are plausibly recoverable from the video's own camera motion. Manipulation, which is what [Index](entities/figure-index.md) is mostly for, has **no published transfer result**. Predates [EgoScale](sources/egoscale-paper.md) by five months and publishes **no dataset size, no success rate, no baseline, no curve**. Also: video captured *"passively as people do behaviors in real Brookfield homes"*, with occupancy and consent unaddressed. (2025-09-18)
- [Figure Announces Strategic Partnership with Brookfield](sources/figure-brookfield-partnership.md) — the business half, one day earlier. **100,000+ residential units, 500M sq ft office, 160M sq ft logistics** against a $1T asset base, used as a training corpus. The wiki's first instance of **real estate monetised as robot training data** — every other strategy here buys *labour*; this one buys **access to space**. Brookfield is simultaneously data supplier, **Series C investor**, and prospective deployment customer, so no strand is priced independently. The GPU-data-center strand is never mentioned again. (2025-09-17)

### Walden Robotics — the humanoid tier's counterargument (ingested 2026-08-28)
- [Walden Robotics Partners With Toyota on Practical Humanoids (IEEE Spectrum)](sources/ieee-spectrum-walden-practical-humanoids.md) — Evan Ackerman interviews [Russ Tedrake](entities/russ-tedrake.md). **Resolves the standing hardware question** on [Walden](entities/walden-robotics.md): a **humanoid torso with two arms and two-finger grippers on a large wheeled base — no legs**. The argument, from the man who taught legged locomotion at MIT for 20 years and led Team MIT's DARPA biped: *"what's the addressable market? And what percentage of it is covered by a wheeled base?"* Three reasons, the first **regulatory, not mechanical** — *"Factories already have safety cases built around AMRs. **You can piggyback on that**"* — plus static stability (it cannot fall over) and a base crammed with batteries that fixes midday power. Grippers chosen on durability, not dexterity: *"at the end of the week, the hands take a beating."* Ackerman pushes back on "general purpose" in his own voice, preferring **"multipurpose."** Publishes **no specifications of any kind** — no height, weight, payload, DOF, battery, price, or even a robot name. (2026-08-03)

### The RT cores that aren't in any spec table (ingested 2026-08-29)

### Neural geometry, EchoJEPA, and a proposed experiment (ingested 2026-08-30)
- [Engels, Michaud, Liao, Gurnee & Tegmark 2024 — Not All Language Model Features Are One-Dimensionally Linear](sources/engels2024-not-all-features-one-dimensionally-linear.md) — MIT/IAIFI; **ICLR 2025**. The **independent, peer-reviewed** challenge to the linear representation hypothesis, 18 months before Goodfire's version and product-free. Defines an **irreducible multi-dimensional feature**, then uses **SAEs to find the structure SAEs miss** by clustering dictionary elements — surfacing **circular** representations of days, months and years in GPT-2 and Mistral 7B (ranked 9th/28th/15th of 1000 clusters by their own automatic test). Causal: patching **only the 2-D circular subspace** ≈ patching the entire layer, in early layers. Honest limitation: prevalence unknown. Also: both large models solve *"two days from Monday"* while failing plain `5+3 (mod 7)`, and GPT-2 **has** the circles and still can't use them — representation and competence come apart in both directions. (2024-05; ICLR 2025)
- [EchoJEPA: A Latent Predictive Foundation Model for Echocardiography](sources/echojepa-paper.md) — Bo Wang's lab (UHN/Vector/Toronto) + Chicago/Philips/UCSF. **[V-JEPA 2](entities/v-jepa-2.md) adapted to echocardiography at 18M videos / 300K patients** — the wiki's largest JEPA-family model outside natural video, and the cleanest test of the JEPA thesis, because ultrasound speckle is *physically* unpredictable: "reconstruction models must faithfully reproduce speckle to minimize their loss." **78.6% with 1% of labels vs 42.1% for the best baseline at 100%**; **2.3% degradation** under acoustic perturbation vs **16.8%** next best; zero-shot adult→pediatric beating all *fine-tuned* baselines. **EchoJEPA-L open-sourced.** Its §3.5 **physics-informed, severity-swept perturbation protocol** is better built than anything in the robot-evaluation literature here. (2026-02-04)

### Goodfire — interpretability as an industry (ingested 2026-08-30)
- [Silico for Robotics & Vision (Goodfire product page)](sources/goodfire-silico-robotics-vision.md) — ⚠️ **vendor marketing, no numbers**. Worth a page for the framing: *"Vision and robotics models often fail in the real world because they learned brittle shortcuts instead of generalizable concepts."* Claims to **validate** (catch generalization failure pre-deployment by reading the latent space), **discover** (attribute a failure back to the training sequences that caused it), and **design** (steer between latent modes without retraining) — which map exactly onto three problems this wiki has documented with no method attached: [LIBERO-PRO](sources/libero-pro-paper.md)'s memorization finding, the [data-engine targeting gap](sources/karpathy-software-3-and-transformer-history-lecture.md), and VLA fine-tuning cost. Robotics case study **names no model**. The credible item is the other one: **ECG signal leakage** caught in **EchoJEPA**, a [JEPA](concepts/world-models/jepa.md)-family echocardiography model the wiki did not know existed. (fetched 2026-08-30)
- [Goodfire research index (Sept 2024 – Aug 2026)](sources/goodfire-research-index.md) — catalog record of ~40 items, half "Fundamental" and half "Applied." The one read: **The World Inside Neural Networks** (Geiger et al., May 2026) — *"even when a model has learned a scalar concept like position, that concept may live on a **curved manifold** rather than along a straight line in activation space,"* with linear steering producing teleportation artifacts where manifold steering is smooth. **A challenge to the linear-representation hypothesis underneath SAEs**, flagged not adopted (Goodfire sells "neural geometry" as a product). Includes an **image-action model on mountain car** — the corpus's only robotics-adjacent work, and a toy. (2024-09 → 2026-08)
- [Goodfire Series B (Feb 2026)](sources/goodfire-series-b.md) — **$150M at $1.25B**, led by B Capital; Eric Schmidt, Salesforce Ventures, DFJ Growth. Ingested as the primary for the funding figures. The recordable claim is the positioning, not the valuation: interpretability as *"the toolset for a new domain of science,"* sold to model builders outside frontier labs, with robotics named as a vertical. (2026-02-05)

- [NVIDIA Jetson Orin module datasheets (AGX Orin DS-10662, Orin NX DS-10712)](sources/nvidia-jetson-orin-module-datasheets.md) — filed after a question about what an RT core is. **NVIDIA's Jetson product-comparison page — the table every Jetson spec in this wiki came from — never mentions RT cores.** The datasheets do: *"Each TPC includes two SMs, a Polymorph Engine, two Texture Units, and a **Ray Tracing core (RTcore)**,"* which "assists Ray Tracing by accelerating **Bounding Volume Hierarchy (BVH) traversal** and intersection of scene geometry." So [AGX Orin](entities/jetson-agx-orin.md) has **8 RT cores** on the 64 GB (2 GPC | 8 TPC) and 7 on the 32 GB — at **half desktop Ampere's density** (1 per TPC, i.e. per 2 SMs, vs 1 per SM). The [Orin NX](entities/jetson-orin-nx.md) count is **not published by any primary**. **The inverted primary-source lesson**: normally the secondary omits what the primary has; here NVIDIA's own summary page omits what NVIDIA's own datasheet documents, because the summary is pitched at AI buyers and RT cores read as graphics. **Absence from a spec table is not absence from the silicon.** Also corroborates the wiki's DLA-share arithmetic exactly — **78 dense INT8 TOPS on a part sold as 157**. (2024-12 / 2022)

## Sources (pedagogical / curriculum companions, undated)
- [Object-Detection-Metrics (Rafael Padilla)](sources/padilla-object-detection-metrics.md) — MIT toolkit whose README is the clearest free walk-through of **detection** metrics: how TP/FP/FN come from **matching** (not a fixed confusion matrix), the confidence-swept PR curve, and 11-point vs all-point AP (26.84% vs 24.56% on the same toy set). The external companion to [pinball_tracker](sources/pinball-tracker-repo.md)'s `docs/EVALUATION.md`; same greedy-under-threshold structure, IoU where pinball uses a distance tolerance. (2018)
- [COCO Detection Evaluation (cocoeval)](sources/coco-detection-eval.md) — the de-facto detection standard: a **12-metric panel**, AP averaged over **IoU 0.50:0.95** (10 thresholds), **101-point** interpolation, `maxDets` 1/10/100, small/medium/large area buckets. The "grown-up" [AP](concepts/robotics/detection-evaluation-metrics.md) machinery; why pinball reports fixed-cap **F1** (one shipped operating point) rather than AP (all operating points). (2014; cocoapi)
- [Computing Neural Network Gradients (Kevin Clark, CS224n)](sources/clark-computing-nn-gradients.md) — the vectorized-backprop cheat sheet: seven Jacobian identities (incl. `∂J/∂θ = ŷ − y` for softmax-CE), the "gradient shape = parameter shape" convention, and a worked 1-layer-NN backward pass. The matrix/vector step up from [micrograd](sources/karpathy-micrograd.md); on-ramp to [Differentiable Programming](sources/blondel-roulet-differentiable-programming.md). Linked from [Curriculum Module 1](syntheses/curriculum/curriculum-01-neural-networks.md). (2019-01)
- [Welch Labs Illustrated Guide to AI, Vol I (book)](sources/welchlabs-illustrated-guide-to-ai.md) — Stephen Welch's 376-page illustrated textbook (Feb 2026, Revision V15). 9 chapters: perceptron → gradient descent → backprop → deep learning → AlexNet → **neural scaling laws** → **mechanistic interpretability** → **attention (DeepSeek MLA)** → diffusion. Pairs chapter-by-chapter with Welch Labs YouTube videos; code at github.com/stephencwelch/ai_book. **The wiki's first ingested primary source on LLM-side scaling laws, mech-interp, and DeepSeek MLA.** (2026-02)
- [Karpathy — Software 3.0 and the history of the Transformer](sources/karpathy-software-3-and-transformer-history-lecture.md) — ⚠️ **third-party compilation** (channel TechXOps, 2026-08-19) stitching two early-2023 Karpathy talks: a Stanford hackathon keynote and a CS25 guest lecture. Cite for **framing, not for paper claims** (his NPLM paraphrase is wrong on a detail). Carries three things the wiki has nowhere else: the **Software 1.0/2.0/3.0** taxonomy and the Tesla **data-engine** loop (whose load-bearing step — *collect the data the network finds troubling* — is exactly what robot data-collection programmes lack); **[Bahdanau](entities/dzmitry-bahdanau.md)'s email** on inventing attention, and that Bengio named it; and **attention as data-dependent message passing on a directed graph**. Also the Tesla sensor-fusion argument for transformers over ConvNets. (68 min; talks early 2023)
- [Welch Labs — The Perceptron (YouTube, Feb 2025)](sources/welchlabs-perceptron.md) — "ChatGPT is made from 100 million of these." Stephen Welch's pedagogical prequel to the [LeCun $1B Bet video](sources/welchlabs-lecun-1b-bet-against-llms.md); also the video companion to **Ch 1** of [the Illustrated Guide](sources/welchlabs-illustrated-guide-to-ai.md). Rosenblatt 1957 → Mark I (1958) → XOR roadblock (Minsky & Papert 1969) → backprop (Rumelhart/Hinton/Williams 1986) → MLP-at-scale (GPT-3). Recommended-viewing for [Curriculum Module 1](syntheses/curriculum/curriculum-01-neural-networks.md). (2025-02)
- [3Blue1Brown — How might LLMs store facts | Deep Learning Chapter 7](sources/3blue1brown-mlp-in-llms.md) — Grant Sanderson; the MLP / FFN block inside a transformer LLM as a key–value fact-lookup mechanism. Covers up/down projection, ReLU, superposition (Johnson–Lindenstrauss), and the "~2/3 of GPT-3's parameters live in MLPs" arithmetic. Foundation for the interpretability / SAE-feature-decomposition program. Recommended-viewing for [Curriculum Module 3](syntheses/curriculum/curriculum-03-attention-and-transformers.md). (2024-08-31)
- [fast.ai — Practical Deep Learning for Coders 2022](sources/fastai-practical-deep-learning.md) — Jeremy Howard; 9-lesson, library-first PyTorch + fastai + Hugging Face Transformers + Gradio onboarding. Strongest "first-touch" pedagogical companion *before* [Curriculum Module 1](syntheses/curriculum/curriculum-01-neural-networks.md) for readers without a year of DL programming. (2022)
- [Cameron R. Wolfe — Understanding and Using SFT for Language Models](sources/wolfe-sft-blog.md) — *Deep (Learning) Focus* Substack, Sep 2023. Three-stage alignment (Pretrain → SFT → RLHF); LIMA's "1,000 examples sufficient" finding; survey of LLaMA-2 / Falcon / MPT / Alpaca / Vicuna / Orca / WizardLM. The theory-side companion to [HF TRL SFT Trainer docs](sources/huggingface-trl-sft-trainer.md). (2023-09-11)
- [Hugging Face TRL — SFT Trainer documentation](sources/huggingface-trl-sft-trainer.md) — the de-facto SFT trainer for LLMs and VLMs in 2026. One-line API; dataset-format dispatch; chat-template auto-application; PEFT/LoRA, Liger Kernel, Unsloth, RapidFire AI integrations; VLM support (Qwen2.5-VL, LLaVA-Instruct-Mix); tool-calling SFT. The implementation companion every wiki-tracked VLA fine-tuning recipe builds on top of. (continuously updated)
- [DS4DS 7.01 — Optimal Control, Introduction (Peitz & Wallscheid)](sources/ds4ds-7-01-optimal-control-intro.md) — Data Science for Dynamical Systems open course (CC BY-SA 4.0; Julia / Jupyter), YouTube Jan 2024. Opening lecture of the 7-lesson module 7 (intro → discrete-time → LQR → LMPC → data-driven MPC via DMD → differential predictive control). The modern-pedagogy companion to [Sussmann & Willems 1997](sources/sussmann-willems-1997-300-years-optimal-control.md) — together they form a complete optimal-control orientation. (2024-01-21)

### The code-as-policy lineage (2022–2024, ingested 2026-08-03)
The nine-paper arc behind [code as policy](concepts/agents/code-as-policy.md), ingested as a block so the 2026 results have their ancestry.

- [Do As I Can, Not As I Say (SayCan)](sources/saycan-paper.md) — CoRL 2022, Robotics at Google + [Everyday Robots](entities/everyday-robots.md). LLM scores skill *relevance*, a learned value function scores *feasibility*, product picks the action. 101 instructions, PaLM 540B: **84% plan / 74% execute** mock kitchen, 81%/60% real. Removing language grounding → **0%**. The skill-selection branch [Code as Policies](sources/code-as-policies-paper.md) defines itself against. (2022-04-04)
- [Inner Monologue](sources/inner-monologue-paper.md) — CoRL 2022. Injects textual feedback (success detection, scene description, human answers) back into the planner. **Under adversarial disturbance SayCan drops to 0% and Inner Monologue holds 60.4%**; object and success feedback are complementary (45%/40% alone, **90%** together). The 2022 origin of "the feedback channel is the bottleneck." (2022-07-12)
- [Code as Policies](sources/code-as-policies-paper.md) — **ICRA 2023, the seminal paper.** LLMs write robot policy code with control flow, third-party libraries, and recursive function definition (hierarchical code-gen: HumanEval P@1 34.9 → **39.8**). Beats a 30k-demo [CLIPort](entities/cliport.md) **62–80% vs 0.00%** out-of-distribution — the same shape as [LIBERO-PRO](sources/libero-pro-paper.md) four years later. **Its own Discussion names the primitive-scope ceiling [CaP-X](sources/cap-x-paper.md) later measures.** (2022-09-16)
- [VoxPoser](sources/voxposer-paper.md) — CoRL 2023, Stanford. LLM writes code composing **3D value maps** a motion planner optimizes. Real world **88%** (70% under disturbance) vs a CaP-style primitives baseline at 24% (**0%** under disturbance); wins all six seen/unseen cells in sim. Zero-shot trajectories as exploration priors learn contact-rich dynamics in **<3 min** vs >12 h. (2023-07-12)
- [Language to Rewards](sources/language-to-rewards-paper.md) — CoRL 2023, Google DeepMind. LLM writes **reward functions** optimized live by MuJoCo MPC. **90% of 17 tasks vs 50%** for a CaP baseline, which "fails to generalize to novel low-level skills." (2023-06-14)
- [Voyager](sources/voyager-paper.md) — TMLR 2024, [GEAR](entities/nvidia-gear.md). Lifelong Minecraft agent: automatic curriculum + **ever-growing executable-code skill library** + self-verification. 3.3× items, 15.3× faster tech tree, only agent to reach diamond. **Handing its library to AutoGPT takes that agent from 0/3 to solving 3 of 4 tasks.** [ASPIRE](entities/aspire.md)'s direct ancestor — same lab, shared authors. Flagged **cost and frontier-model dependence in 2023**; both still unsolved. (2023-05-25)
- [CodeAct](sources/codeact-paper.md) — ICML 2024, UIUC. Code as the general LLM-agent action space. Across 17 LLMs, code wins **12/17 on multi-tool composition** but only marginally on atomic tool calls — the advantage is *control flow*, not serialization. +20.7 pp for the best model. (2024-02-01)

## Sources (foundational, out of chronological order)
- [Sussmann & Willems 1997 — 300 Years of Optimal Control: From the Brachystochrone to the Maximum Principle](sources/sussmann-willems-1997-300-years-optimal-control.md) — Rutgers / Groningen; IEEE Control Systems Magazine "Historical Perspectives," June 1997. Tercentenary essay arguing optimal control was born in 1697 with Bernoulli's brachystochrone solution — not in 1956 with Pontryagin. Distinguishes OC ⊋ CoV: dynamical constraints `q̇ = f(q, u, t)` + control-set constraints `u ∈ U` are the structural additions. Walks the canonical chain Bernoulli → Euler–Lagrange → Hamilton → Jacobi → Weierstrass → Pontryagin's Maximum Principle. The wiki's primary-source anchor for the optimal-control machinery underneath MPC / CEM / TD-MPC / learned-world-model planning. (1997-06)
- [Barlow 1961 — Possible Principles Underlying the Transformations of Sensory Messages](sources/barlow1961-sensory-messages.md) — Horace Barlow's foundational neuroscience paper introducing the redundancy-reduction principle (recode redundant sensory input into a factorial code with statistically independent components). Eponymous source for Barlow Twins (2021). The lineage root for VICReg → SIGReg → DINOv3 Gram anchoring. (1961)
- [Bromley, Guyon, LeCun, Säckinger, Shah 1993 — Signature Verification using a "Siamese" Time Delay Neural Network](sources/bromley1993-siamese-signature-verification.md) — original Siamese network paper, AT&T Bell Labs / NIPS 1993. Two weight-tied TDNN sub-networks + cosine + `±1` targets for genuine vs forgery pairs. The architectural ancestor of every joint-embedding SSL system: Barlow Twins, VICReg, DINOv2/v3, and the J/A in [JEPA](concepts/world-models/jepa.md). LeCun's 1990s precursor to his 2020s JEPA program — same author, same architectural family, different loss. (1993)
- [Bengio, Ducharme, Vincent & Jauvin 2003 — A Neural Probabilistic Language Model](sources/bengio2003-neural-probabilistic-language-model.md) — Université de Montréal; JMLR 3:1137–1155. **Where the learned embedding table comes from.** Give each word a vector `C(i) ∈ R^m` (`m` = 30–100 vs `|V|` ≈ 17k), express next-word probability as a smooth function of the context words' vectors, and **train both at once** — so `The cat is walking in the bedroom` lifts `A dog was running in a room`. `y = b + Wx + U tanh(d + Hx)` + softmax; params **linear** in `|V|` and `n`, against the `n`-gram's exponential. Brown test perplexity **252** vs **312** for the best `n`-gram (24%) and **336** for deleted interpolation (33%); AP News **109** vs **117**. Measured **99.7% of compute in the output softmax** and spent three weeks on 40 CPUs — the bottleneck that produced hierarchical softmax and negative sampling. §5.1 is the wiki's earliest [EBM](concepts/learning/energy-based-models.md); §5.2 predicts hierarchical softmax, sampled training, embedding interpretability, and contextual embeddings — everything except attention. (2003-02)
- [Mikolov, Chen, Corrado & Dean 2013 — Efficient Estimation of Word Representations in Vector Space](sources/mikolov2013-efficient-estimation-word-representations.md) — Google; ICLR 2013 workshop. **word2vec, paper 1 of 2.** Deletes the NNLM's non-linear hidden layer to get **CBOW** and **Skip-gram**, log-linear models cheap enough to train at a scale the better model can't reach — Skip-gram 1000-d on 6B words hits **65.6%** analogy accuracy in 2.5 days × 125 cores against the NNLM's 50.8% in **14 days × 180**. Introduces the **analogy benchmark** (19,544 questions) that made embedding geometry measurable, and the dimensionality × data table showing **neither axis works alone**. CBOW wins syntax, Skip-gram wins semantics 2.3×. ⚠️ The famous `king − man + woman` search **excludes the input words**, and Table 8's showcase contains visible misses. Also the step where embeddings became a **downloadable product** (100B+ words, 1.4M entity vectors). (2013-01)
- [Mikolov, Sutskever, Chen, Corrado & Dean 2013 — Distributed Representations of Words and Phrases and their Compositionality](sources/mikolov2013-distributed-representations-words-phrases.md) — Google; NIPS 2013. **word2vec, paper 2 of 2.** Kills the softmax with **negative sampling** — a deliberate simplification of [NCE](glossary.md#nce) that *drops the guarantee* of approximately maximizing the softmax log-likelihood, on the stated grounds that only representation quality matters; the template for InfoNCE and the whole contrastive-[SSL](syntheses/curriculum/curriculum-04-self-supervised-learning.md) line. Plus **subsampling** (`1 − √(t/f(w))`, 2–10× faster *and* better rare-word vectors), phrase tokens, and the mechanism behind additive compositionality (vector sum ≈ product of context distributions ≈ AND). ⚠️ **"Negative sampling is the word2vec method" is folklore** — the paper's own best model (72% on phrase analogies) uses hierarchical softmax. (2013-10)
- [Cho, van Merriënboer, Gulcehre, Bahdanau, Bougares, Schwenk & Bengio 2014 — Learning Phrase Representations using RNN Encoder–Decoder](sources/cho2014-rnn-encoder-decoder-phrase-representations.md) — Montréal + Jacobs Bremen + Le Mans; EMNLP 2014. **The GRU's primary source, and the encoder–decoder's.** A reset gate and an update gate, no memory cell — two gates against the LSTM's four plus a cell — and the paper never uses the name "GRU" (§2.3 is *"Hidden Unit that Adaptively Remembers and Forgets"*). ⚠️ Its published update gate has the **opposite polarity to PyTorch's**. Used not as a translator but as **one feature scoring phrase pairs inside Moses**: 33.30 → 33.87 alone, **34.64** with a neural LM. Deliberately trained **without phrase frequencies**, so capacity goes to linguistic regularity rather than corpus counts — and it shows, in a table where the count-based model degenerates into mojibake on rare phrases while this one stays clean. Figure 5 is the wiki's first **phrase**-level embedding result. **Bahdanau is the fourth author**, three months from overturning it. (2014-06)
- [Sutskever, Vinyals & Le 2014 — Sequence to Sequence Learning with Neural Networks](sources/sutskever2014-sequence-to-sequence-learning.md) — Google; NIPS 2014. The **encoder–decoder**: one 4-layer LSTM compresses the source into **8,000 numbers**, a second decodes autoregressively to `<EOS>`. First pure neural MT to beat phrase-based SMT (**34.81 vs 33.30** BLEU; 36.5 rescoring). **Reversing the source sentence** is worth ~4.7 BLEU with no model change — more than a 5× ensemble — and is a workaround for the **fixed-vector bottleneck**, not a fix. Beam 1 nearly suffices; the oracle rescoring line (~45) shows how much the 36.5 left behind. The shape survives the architecture: encode an observation, decode a horizon — every action-chunking policy in this wiki. (2014-09)
- [Cho, van Merriënboer, Bahdanau & Bengio 2014 — On the Properties of Neural Machine Translation](sources/cho2014b-properties-of-neural-machine-translation.md) — Montréal + Jacobs Bremen; SSST-8 workshop. **The measurement behind attention's conjecture**, and an *analysis* paper rather than an architecture one — its stated aim is understanding "the **[inductive bias](concepts/learning/inductive-bias.md)** of the encoder–decoder approach." BLEU collapses with sentence length and with unknown-word count, against a Moses baseline whose BLEU *rises* with length (Fig. 5 — the contrast is what turns a limitation into a diagnosis). ⚠️ **Two things the standard citation hides**: it was posted **2 days *after*** the attention paper it motivates (1 Sep vs 3 Sep 2014), and having tested two unrelated encoders and found both failing identically, its §6 blames the **decoder**, not the fixed-length vector. Also: the 30k vocabulary costs *more* than the length effect (13.92 → 23.45 BLEU just by excluding unknown-word sentences). Introduces **grConv**, which does unsupervised parsing as a byproduct. (2014-09)
- [Bahdanau, Cho & Bengio 2014 — Neural Machine Translation by Jointly Learning to Align and Translate](sources/bahdanau2014-neural-machine-translation-align-translate.md) — Jacobs Bremen + Montréal; ICLR 2015. **The attention paper.** Names the fixed-length-vector bottleneck and removes it: bidirectional encoder → one annotation per source position → a **distinct context vector per target word**, `c_i = Σ_j α_ij h_j` with `α` a softmax over a jointly-trained alignment net. The deep move is making alignment a **soft differentiable weighting instead of a latent variable inferred by EM**. +7.6 BLEU over the same group's encoder–decoder, and **36.15 vs Moses' 35.63** on the no-UNK subset — while Moses gets an extra 418M-word monolingual corpus. Figure 2 (flat BLEU-vs-length where the baseline collapses) is the real evidence. Named by Bengio; the working name was *RNNsearch*. (2014-09)
- [Vaswani et al. 2017 — Attention Is All You Need](sources/attention-is-all-you-need.md) — the Transformer paper. NeurIPS 2017; Google Brain / Google Research. Sequence transduction built entirely on attention, no recurrence, no convolution. Encoder–decoder, multi-head scaled dot-product attention, sinusoidal positional encoding, `h=8`, `d_model=512`, `N=6`. 28.4 BLEU EN-DE / 41.8 BLEU EN-FR. The foundation of every modern architecture downstream: LLMs, ViTs, VLA action heads, JEPA predictors, BeT / VQ-BeT policies, Diffusion Policy transformer backbones. (2017-06-12)
- [Dosovitskiy et al. 2020 — An Image Is Worth 16x16 Words (ViT)](sources/vit-paper.md) — the Vision Transformer paper. ICLR 2021; Google Research, Brain Team. Patch tokenization + learned positional embedding + `[CLS]` token + standard transformer encoder = first pure-attention vision model. Pre-trained on JFT-300M, ViT-H/14 hits 88.55% ImageNet top-1 at 2–4× less compute than BiT-L / Noisy Student. Central claim: **at scale, data trumps inductive bias.** The backbone underneath every ViT-encoder in this wiki — DINOv2, DINOv3, V-JEPA 2, LeWM, DINO-WM, DINO-world, JEPA-WMs, LeJEPA, PLDM. (2020-10-22)
- [Wake-Sleep Paper (Hinton, Dayan, Frey, Neal, Science 1995)](sources/wake-sleep-paper.md) — University of Toronto. Bottom-up **recognition** + top-down **generative** connections; wake phase trains generative weights, sleep phase trains recognition weights on model-generated "fantasies"; MDL/Helmholtz-free-energy objective (coins **"Helmholtz machine"**). The recognition-model ancestor of the [VAE](sources/vae-paper.md) — with the two flaws (inconsistent objectives, wrong-direction KL) the VAE fixed. Digits: 4.8% error vs 6.7% NN / 5.6% supervised backprop. (1995-05)
- [Stochastic Backpropagation Paper (Rezende, Mohamed, Wierstra, ICML 2014)](sources/stochastic-backpropagation-paper.md) — Google DeepMind (arxiv 1401.4082). The paper **co-credited with Kingma & Welling for the VAE**, developed concurrently and independently: deep latent Gaussian models (DLGMs), Gaussian gradient identities (Bonnet/Price) + location-scale reparameterization, **O(1)-vs-O(K) variance** argument against REINFORCE, rank-1-corrected posteriors, "amortised inference" framing. Binarized MNIST NLL 86.60 vs [wake-sleep](sources/wake-sleep-paper.md) 91.3. (2014-01)
- [VAE Paper — Auto-Encoding Variational Bayes (Kingma & Welling, ICLR 2014)](sources/vae-paper.md) — Universiteit van Amsterdam (arxiv 1312.6114). The variational autoencoder: SGVB estimator (**reparameterization trick** `z = μ + σε` makes the ELBO differentiable), AEVB algorithm (**amortized inference** via a neural recognition model / probabilistic encoder), closed-form Gaussian KL; beats wake-sleep + Monte Carlo EM on MNIST / Frey Face. Substrate of the [DDPM](entities/ddpm.md) variational bound, [ACT](entities/act.md)'s CVAE, [VQ-BeT](entities/vq-bet.md)'s VQ-VAE tokenizer, and latent-diffusion latent spaces. (2013-12; v11 2022-12)
- [β-VAE Paper (Higgins et al., ICLR 2017)](sources/beta-vae-paper.md) — Google DeepMind. Weight the [VAE](sources/vae-paper.md) KL term by **β > 1** (KKT relaxation of a constrained objective) → **disentangled latents** (single units ↔ single generative factors); introduces the first quantitative disentanglement metric + the 2D-shapes dataset (dSprites ancestor); **99.23%** vs VAE 61.58% / InfoGAN 73.5%, matches semi-supervised DC-IGN unsupervised. Makes the reconstruction-vs-disentanglement trade-off explicit; the KL-weighting knob in downstream CVAE/VAE components. (2017)
- [Sutton & Barto — Reinforcement Learning: An Introduction (2nd ed., MIT Press 2018 / 2020 reprint)](sources/sutton-barto-rl-textbook.md) — UMass / UAlberta / DeepMind; A Bradford / MIT Press; ISBN 9780262039246; CC BY-NC-ND 2.0 electronic. The canonical RL textbook (548 pp). Defines the field's four-subelement decomposition (policy / reward / value function / model) and the unifying narrative MC ↔ TD ↔ DP via Bellman bootstrapping. **Ch 13 (Policy Gradient Methods)** is the lineage of REINFORCE → Actor-Critic → PPO / SAC / GRPO. **Ch 16 (Applications)** covers DQN/Atari (Mnih 2015) + AlphaGo/AlphaGo Zero (Silver 2016/2017). **Ch 11 (Deadly Triad)** is the cleanest theoretical diagnosis of why deep-RL training is fragile. The primary-source anchor for [Module 8 — RL vocabulary](syntheses/curriculum/curriculum-08-rl-vocabulary.md), every MBRL paper ([DreamerV3](sources/dreamer-v3-paper.md), [TD-MPC2](sources/td-mpc2-paper.md)), every learned-WM thread, and the RLHF/DPO/GRPO line underneath every VLA. The "RL = approximate optimal control under uncertainty" bridge to [Sussmann & Willems 1997](sources/sussmann-willems-1997-300-years-optimal-control.md). Sutton + Barto won the 2024 Turing Award for the work consolidated in this book. (2018 final 2nd ed.; 2014–2015 in-progress draft also on file for historical reference)
- [Barlow Twins Paper](sources/barlow-twins-paper.md) — Zbontar, Jing, Misra, LeCun, Deny (FAIR + NYU; ICML 2021, arxiv 2103.03230). First non-asymmetric anti-collapse SSL method: cross-correlation between two augmented views' embeddings → identity. No predictor, no momentum encoder, no stop-gradient. ImageNet linear top-1 73.2%. Names itself after Horace Barlow's redundancy-reduction principle. (2021-03-04)
- [VICReg Paper](sources/vicreg-paper.md) — Bardes, Ponce, LeCun (FAIR + Inria + NYU; ICLR 2022, arxiv 2105.04906). Three-term anti-collapse loss: variance hinge + covariance decorrelation + invariance MSE. Branches need not share weights or architecture — natural multi-modal SSL. The regularizer LeCun's AMI paper cites by name as the JEPA anti-collapse method; methodological precursor to SIGReg / LeJEPA. (2021-05-11)
- [LeCun 2022 — A Path Towards Autonomous Machine Intelligence](sources/lecun2022-path-towards-ami.md) — LeCun's position paper. Defines the JEPA / H-JEPA architecture, the configurable world model + configurator framing, intrinsic-cost + critic, and the EBM training story behind all subsequent JEPA papers. The vision document AMI Labs was founded to execute. (2022-06-27)
- [DINOv3 Paper](sources/dinov3-paper.md) — Siméoni et al., Meta AI Research (arxiv 2508.10104). 7B-parameter ViT SSL foundation model; introduces Gram anchoring (regularize patch-similarity structure toward an earlier "Gram teacher") to fix the long-training dense-feature degradation observed in DINOv2 at scale. Frozen-backbone COCO mAP 66.1; ADE20k mIoU 63.0. New SSL state-of-the-art and natural drop-in upgrade for the DINO-WM / DINO-world / JEPA-WMs lineage. (2025-08-13)
- [Reinforcement Learning in Robotics: A Survey (Kober, Bagnell & Peters, IJRR 2013)](sources/kober-rl-robotics-survey-2013.md) — the canonical pre-deep-learning survey of RL on real robots. The **four curses** (dimensionality / real-world samples / under-modeling / goal specification); why policy search beat value functions on hardware; **"demonstrations remove global exploration"** (prophetic of today's BC-dominated field); ball-in-a-cup case study (217-param DMP, PoWER, ~75 episodes). Sits between [Sutton & Barto](sources/sutton-barto-rl-textbook.md) and the modern demonstration-dominated robot-learning line; companion to [optimal control](concepts/robotics/optimal-control.md). (2013)

## Entities

### Companies
- [EchoJEPA](entities/echojepa.md) — [V-JEPA 2](entities/v-jepa-2.md) adapted to echocardiography (18M videos, 300K patients); ViT-Giant 1.1B proprietary + **ViT-Large 307M open-sourced** on MIMIC-IV-Echo. The wiki's clinical branch of the [JEPA](concepts/world-models/jepa.md) family. 1% of labels beats the best baseline at 100%. (2 sources)
- [Goodfire](entities/goodfire.md) — San Francisco public benefit corporation selling **interpretability as a product**; **$150M Series B at $1.25B** (Feb 2026, B Capital), founded by DeepMind/OpenAI interpretability alumni. Its product **Silico** is an *interpretability agent* — auto-interp, SAE and probe training, model diffing, causal analysis, data attribution, neural geometry — with **Robotics & Vision as one of three named verticals**. The wiki's first evidence that mech-interp has become an industry rather than a frontier-lab safety programme, and the first source proposing to debug **robot-policy internals** rather than success rates. ⚠️ The robotics case study names no model and reports no numbers, and the 40-item research corpus contains **no robotics research at all**. (3 sources)
- [Brookfield](entities/brookfield.md) — >$1T AUM asset manager and one of the world's largest real-estate owners; in this wiki as [Figure](entities/figure.md)'s **human-video data supplier**. Its **100,000+ residential units / 500M sq ft office / 160M sq ft logistics** are the collection sites for [Project Go-Big](sources/figure-project-go-big.md). The first case here of a **property portfolio priced as a robotics data asset** — what Figure buys is environment diversity no lab can stage. Also a Series C investor and prospective deployment site, so supplier, financier and customer are one party. Consent inside occupied dwellings goes unaddressed in both announcements. (2 sources)
- [BotQ](entities/botq.md) — [Figure](entities/figure.md)'s in-house humanoid factory, and the reason [Figure 03](entities/figure-03.md) looks the way it does: the robot moved from **CNC machining to die-casting/stamping/injection moulding** to suit the line. Nameplate **12,000 humanoids/year**, goal 100,000 over four years; measured April 2026 at **350+ units delivered**, **1/day → 1/hour (24×)**, **80%+ end-of-line first-pass yield**, 99.3% on batteries, 9,000+ actuators. The first humanoid facility in this wiki to publish yields and cycle time at all — and on current public evidence, **the widest moat in the humanoid tier is a manufacturing one, not an AI one**. (3 sources)
- [Rhoban](entities/rhoban.md) — autonomous-robotics group of **LaBRI** (Bordeaux University / ENSEIRB-MATMECA / CNRS); **four-time RoboCup champion** in the humanoid league. Enters this wiki through its *tooling* rather than its research: **BAM** (servo friction identification — the M6 model of the [Dynamixel](entities/dynamixel.md) XL330 behind [Microduck](entities/microduck.md)'s [actuator fidelity](concepts/learning/actuator-fidelity-sim2real.md)) and **`onshape-to-robot`** (CAD → URDF/SDF/MuJoCo). Third node in a **Bordeaux cluster** with [Pollen Robotics](entities/pollen-robotics.md) and [Open Duck Mini](entities/open-duck-mini.md) — Antoine Pirrone belongs to both. A university RoboCup lab supplied a load-bearing piece of the $399-robot story. (2 sources)
- [Dexai Robotics](entities/dexai-robotics.md) — Boston food-preparation robotics (Draper spin-out, 2018; *Alfred*, utensils as end effectors, robotics-as-a-service). **The wiki's only confirmed production user of [GCS](concepts/robotics/graphs-of-convex-sets.md)** — it replaced a tuned **PRM** planner, in a fixed workcell making thousands of plans a day where cycle time is revenue. The datapoint that turns GCS from an elegant result into a deployed one, and the one that shows how narrow the deployed regime is. (2 sources)
- [Flexion Robotics](entities/flexion.md) — Zürich; *"the **autonomy stack** for humanoid robots… across any hardware and task"*, via **simulation + RL with minimal human involvement**. Sells policies and deployment software, not robots. Owns the sim2real half of the [Niantic/NVIDIA collaboration](sources/niantic-flexion-nvidia-sim2real.md) — RGB-only navigation, zero-shot to a real humanoid. **A data-light bet against the demonstration-collection economics the rest of this wiki tracks** — but the evidence covers **navigation only**, and the demo policy is deliberately **site-specific**, which sits awkwardly beside "any hardware and task." (1 source)
- [Niantic Spatial](entities/niantic-spatial.md) — Pokémon-Go spinout selling *"the infrastructure layer for physical AI"*; Large Geospatial Model, on-device Gaussian splats, open **SPZ** format, and the visual-relocalization research group (Brachmann/ACE, Prisacariu, Brostow). Robotics listed first among industries, **with no robotics evidence published**. (1 source)
- [Robbyant (Ant Group robotics)](entities/robbyant.md) — publishes the **LingBot** family: **VLA** (policy) + **World** (world model, 5th/10 on WorldRoamBench) + **[Map](entities/lingbot-map.md)** (reconstruction) + **[UME](entities/ume.md)** (teleop hardware). **Policy + world model + map + data collection from one company, published openly** — a span matched here only by [NVIDIA GEAR](entities/nvidia-gear.md) and [Physical Intelligence](entities/physical-intelligence.md), neither of which ships an open exoskeleton. (3 sources)
- [Dimensional Inc.](entities/dimensional-inc.md) — the company behind [DimOS](entities/dimos.md); Apache-2.0 open-core with a hosted commercial surface (**dimTELE** WebRTC teleop brokering). Founder/funding/headcount **not established by any ingested source** — the artifact is inspectable, the company is not. (1 source)
- [OpenAI](entities/openai.md) — frontier lab, present in this wiki almost entirely through **agent security**: origin of the [2026-07-21 evaluation sandbox escape](sources/openai-hugging-face-eval-security-incident.md) that set off the summer-2026 disclosure cascade, and supplier of Codex (a harness) and GPT-5.6 Sol (named in two of the four incidents). **Its own pages are unreachable from this environment** — 403/Cloudflare — so every OpenAI claim here is sourced from other parties' first-party accounts. (4 sources)
- [UK AI Security Institute (AISI)](entities/uk-aisi.md) — UK government frontier-model evaluator; builds **Inspect**. Tests *"under deliberately permissive conditions: with access to the open internet, and with some safety filters disabled"* to measure maximum capability — the posture you must know before reading anything it publishes. Author of [INC-2026-07-28-01](sources/aisi-incident-report-unsanctioned-agent-behaviour.md), the most detailed and least self-serving of the 2026 incident reports: detected by commercial monitoring, **contained in under an hour**, models disabled fleet-wide, GitHub notified, METR engaged, 35-page report out within a week, and an audit opened of past evaluations for behaviour that went unnoticed. (2 sources)
- [Nori Robotics](entities/nori-robotics.md) — San Francisco, **YC S26**, 5 people, founded 2026 by Antonio Sitong Li (Columbia; prior research on teaching robots from VR demonstrations). Sells the **[NORI A3](entities/nori-a3.md)** at **$1,688** — the first product in this wiki's sub-$2k tier with a complete published price, a shipped unit, and self-reported revenue (>$300 K in 6 weeks). Frames the robot explicitly as a **data-collection instrument**: subsidise the collector, harvest household demonstrations via a **Skills Marketplace**, train generalist policies. No repository, no licence, no named model. (2 sources)
- [Vulcan Robotics](entities/vulcan-robotics.md) — US startup ([Nick Maselli](entities/nick-maselli.md)) behind [Sourccey](entities/sourccey.md); hardware **CERN-OHL-S** copyleft while software is Apache 2.0; business model is hardware **plus rented inference compute** — a candid concession that the robot's advertised AI doesn't run on the robot. (2 sources)
- [XPENG Robotics](entities/xpeng-robotics.md) — robotics arm of the Chinese EV maker; lead affiliation on [UniT](entities/unit.md); the IRON-R01-1.11 humanoid has a **50-dimensional** action space. (1 source) _stub_
- [Huawei](entities/huawei.md) — industrial-research co-author on [TurboVLA](entities/turbovla.md) with [HUST](entities/hust.md); no wiki coverage yet of its own robotics hardware or Ascend as a VLA inference target. (1 source) _stub_
- [HUST](entities/hust.md) — Huazhong University of Science and Technology; [Xiang Bai](entities/xiang-bai.md)'s CV group, publishing embodied AI as **H-EmbodVis**. Reached a top-tier LIBERO result **on four RTX 4090s**. (1 source)
- [Ai2 (Allen Institute for AI)](entities/ai2.md) — Seattle nonprofit AI lab (Paul Allen, 2014); tagline **"Truly open breakthrough AI"**; three mission pillars — **AI for Science** ([Asta](entities/asta.md)), **AI for the Planet** ([OlmoEarth](entities/olmoearth.md)), **Embodied AI** ([MolmoAct2](entities/molmoact2.md)) — atop the open [OLMo](entities/olmo.md)/[OLMoE](entities/olmoe.md) LLMs, [Dolma](entities/dolma.md) + PixMo data, and [Molmo](entities/molmo.md) VLMs. CEO **Ali Farhadi** co-authored the original [YOLO](sources/yolo-you-only-look-once-2016.md). (11 sources)
- [Agile Robots](entities/agile-robots.md) — Munich; DLR-spinout industrial-automation + Physical-AI company (€200M rev, ~2,000 staff); maker of the **[Agile ONE](entities/agile-one.md)** industrial humanoid + FR3/Diana arms + AMRs under the AgileCore platform; **integration-not-standalone** humanoid strategy, built in Bavaria. First European industrial humanoid in the wiki. (2 sources)
- [Techman Robot](entities/techman-robot.md) — Taiwan; Quanta-subsidiary **[cobot](concepts/robotics/collaborative-robots.md)** maker; first cobots with built-in vision; world's #2 cobot brand (~10% vs Universal Robots ~50%); Omron 10% stake. The classical industrial-automation contrast to the wiki's VLA center of gravity. (1 source)
- [EngineAI](entities/engineai.md) — Shenzhen humanoid company (founded Oct 2023; ~110 staff, >$160M raised); highly-dynamic bipeds with self-developed joints (SA01 / SE01 / PM01 front-flip / **[T800](entities/engineai-t800.md)**); organizer of the [URKL](sources/urkl-robot-combat-league.md) combat league. (2 sources)
- [Walden Robotics](entities/walden-robotics.md) — Cambridge MA; [Russ Tedrake](entities/russ-tedrake.md)-led [TRI](entities/tri.md) spin-out (Jan 2026, launched 2026-07-15); **$300M seed / $1.1B**, Toyota-co-led; general-purpose **manufacturing/logistics** robots on **[LBMs](concepts/learning/large-behavior-models.md) + [Diffusion Policy](entities/diffusion-policy.md)**; in production at a Toyota NC plant since Feb 2026. The academic-LBM-line → commercial bridge. **Hardware resolved 2026-08-28**: a **humanoid torso with two arms and two-finger grippers on a wheeled base — no legs**, chosen to inherit the AMR safety case rather than write a new one. The wiki's sharpest argument that **the legs are the optional part of a humanoid**, made by the man who taught legged locomotion at MIT for 20 years. Publishes **no specifications at all**. (4 sources)
- [BeingBeyond](entities/beingbeyond.md) — Peking-Univ-tied ([Zongqing Lu](entities/zongqing-lu.md)) humanoid **[whole-body control](concepts/robotics/whole-body-control.md)** group; source of [BumbleBee](sources/bumblebee-experts-to-generalist-wbc.md) (expert→generalist WBC, SOTA on [Unitree G1](entities/unitree-g1.md)); the China / data-level counterweight to [NVIDIA GEAR](entities/nvidia-gear.md)'s model-scaling WBC line. (1 source)
- [Alias Robotics](entities/alias-robotics.md) — Spanish **robot-cybersecurity** company; maintainer of the open [Robot Security Framework (RSF)](sources/aliasrobotics-rsf-github.md); the wiki's entry into classical robot security (OS/middleware/network/firmware on [ROS 2](entities/ros2.md)). (1 source)
- [Sensori Robotics](entities/sensori-robotics.md) — Southlake TX; sells the [Yuri](entities/yuri.md) integrated bimanual Physical-AI rig; open OpenArm+/OpenBase hardware; commercial integrator on top of open hardware. (1 source)
- [Deepak Pathak](entities/deepak-pathak.md) — Raj Reddy Associate Professor at CMU **and** co-founder/CEO of [Skild AI](entities/skild-ai.md) (holds both). Co-author of [LocoFormer](sources/locoformer-paper.md); the curiosity-driven-exploration and RMA lines are his, both uningested. (1 source)
- [Abhinav Gupta](entities/abhinav-gupta.md) — CMU Robotics Institute professor, co-founder/president of [Skild AI](entities/skild-ai.md), ex-Meta FAIR Pittsburgh. Self-supervised / lifelong learning at scale. **Not** Abhishek Gupta (UW), who appears on [SERL](sources/serl-paper.md) and [RoboArena](sources/roboarena-paper.md). (0 sources — known-thin)
- [Skild AI](entities/skild-ai.md) — Pittsburgh robot-foundation-model company (CMU spinout 2023, Deepak Pathak + Abhinav Gupta); the **Skild Brain**, positioned "omni-bodied." Flagship **[S1](sources/skild-s1-blog.md)** conditions on a **demonstration video at inference** rather than language. Series C ~$1.4B at **>$14B** (`[live-web]`), anchored by industrial strategics (Samsung, LG, Schneider Electric, NVIDIA). Note the gap: the site says "any robot for any task"; S1's announcement **names no embodiment and makes no cross-embodiment claim**. (1 source)
- [Stanford HAI](entities/stanford-hai.md) — Stanford Institute for Human-Centered AI; publisher of the [AI Index](sources/stanford-hai-ai-index-2026.md) (the wiki's canonical 89.4% sim vs **12.4%** [BEHAVIOR-1K](entities/behavior-benchmark.md) gap figure) and of the 2026 [world-model policy brief](sources/hai-world-model-spatial-intelligence-brief.md). The AI Index measures; the Issue Brief prescribes. Discloses funding from [NVIDIA](entities/nvidia.md) and Google. (2 sources)
- [World Labs](entities/world-labs.md) — founded by [Fei-Fei Li](entities/fei-fei-li.md) (CEO) with **Justin Johnson, Christoph Lassner, Ben Mildenhall**. Author of the renderer/simulator/planner [taxonomy](concepts/world-models/world-model-functional-taxonomy.md) and of the argument that **the simulator is the linchpin**. Products: **[Marble](entities/marble.md)** (splats + meshes from one model, first release ~Nov–Dec 2025) and **RTFM** (real-time renderer, no detail anywhere). Over June–July 2026 it moved from renderer to simulator — [acquired SceniX](sources/world-labs-scenix-acquisition.md) 2026-07-21, shipped [R2S2R](sources/world-labs-r2s2r.md) results seven days later. On a robotics foundation model: omni-models with action output, **"we're definitely not ruling this out."** Now bi-coastal (SF + NY). **No funding, headcount, named customer, or benchmark result in any ingested source.** (5 sources)
- [SceniX](entities/scenix.md) — robotics/simulation company acquired by [World Labs](entities/world-labs.md) 2026-07-21; built the **[R2S2R engine](concepts/robotics/real-to-sim-to-real.md)** that turns one physical task into many controllable, reusable simulated worlds for policy training and evaluation. Founded by Columbia professors **[Yunzhu Li](entities/yunzhu-li.md)** (robotics) and **[Changxi Zheng](entities/changxi-zheng.md)** (simulation/graphics) plus a third engineering co-founder whose name the captions destroy. Sold in two tiers (real-to-sim only, or the full pipeline); **embodiment- and model-agnostic**. Entered World Labs as a **paying Marble customer**. (3 sources)
- [Waymo](entities/waymo.md) — Alphabet's autonomous-driving company; author of the [Waymo World Model](sources/waymo-world-model.md) (generative-video AV simulation, built on [Genie 3](entities/genie-3.md)). (1 source)
- [Waddle Labs](entities/waddle-labs.md) — builds **Waddle**, an agent/API that controls robots via **[code as policy](concepts/agents/code-as-policy.md)** (LLM agent decomposes goals, writes control code, calls [VLAs](concepts/learning/vla-models.md) as tools) atop a shared, agent-authored skill library. Positioned against the end-to-end VLA recipe. Company basics (funding, team, location) unstated. (3 sources)
- [UnitedMasters](entities/unitedmasters.md) — music distribution/licensing company; [Jordan](entities/michael-i-jordan.md)'s real-world **three-way collectivist-AI market** (musicians ↔ listeners ↔ brands, incentives built in); >1.5M musicians. (1 source)
- [Standard Bots](entities/standard-bots.md) — US industrial 6-axis arm maker (RO1/Core, $37k); self-serve **learn-by-demonstration** AI platform; the commercial/industrial face of imitation learning. (1 source)
- [NVIDIA](entities/nvidia.md) — owns most of the agentic-robotics simulation substrate; also owns [Brev](entities/nvidia-brev.md) GPU-cloud broker and the in-house [GEAR](entities/nvidia-gear.md) research lab. (47 sources)
- [NVIDIA GEAR](entities/nvidia-gear.md) — Generalist Embodied Agent Research; co-led by [Jim Fan](entities/jim-fan.md) + [Yuke Zhu](entities/yuke-zhu.md); founded Feb 2024; source of GR00T, the Dream*-WM line ([DreamGen](entities/dreamgen.md)/DreamDojo), [FLARE](concepts/world-models/flare.md), [GEAR-SONIC](entities/gear-sonic.md) + [MotionBricks](sources/motionbricks-paper.md) whole-body control, Eureka, and much of the Isaac Lab / RoboCasa / MimicGen substrate. (15 sources)
- [Hiwonder](entities/hiwonder.md) — Chinese educational-robotics vendor; ROSOrin / ROSOrin Pro kits + `openclaw_controller` ROS 2 bridge for OpenClaw. (4 sources)
- [Hailo](entities/hailo.md) — edge-AI NPU vendor; silicon in Raspberry Pi's AI HATs (Hailo-8/8L vision; **Hailo-10H** generative-AI). The wiki's non-CUDA onboard-compute option vs. Jetson. (2 sources)
- [Raspberry Pi 5](entities/raspberry-pi-5.md) — low-cost SBC host/relay in XLeRobot/LeKiwi/Grievous builds; host for the Hailo [AI HAT+ 2](sources/raspberry-pi-ai-hat-plus-2.md). (2 sources)
- [Hugging Face](entities/hugging-face.md) — open-source AI company; maintainer of [LeRobot](entities/lerobot.md); HF Hub hosts model checkpoints across the wiki's JEPA / VLA / IL coverage; July 2026 five-part NVIDIA integration into LeRobot. (15 sources)
- [Nous Research](entities/nous-research.md) — open-source AI org behind the Hermes LLM family and [Hermes Agent](entities/hermes-agent.md) (171K stars, *"most used agent in the world"* per OpenRouter via NVIDIA). (3 sources)
- [SIGRobotics-UIUC](entities/sigrobotics-uiuc.md) — student-led robotics org at UIUC; designs and maintains [LeKiwi](entities/lekiwi.md); won U.S. site of the Oct 2025 Embodied AI Hackathon with a GR00T-driven matcha-making XLeRobot; sponsored by FrodoBots / Hugging Face / K-Scale Labs / Neuralink / ROBOTIS / others. (7 sources)
- [Seeed Studio](entities/seeed-studio.md) — Shenzhen open-hardware distributor; sells LeKiwi and hosts the canonical end-user tutorial; co-organizer of LeRobot 2025 + Embodied AI 2025 hackathons. (8 sources)
- [Connectivity Standards Alliance](entities/connectivity-standards-alliance.md) — standards body behind [Matter](entities/matter.md) (formerly Zigbee Alliance); the wiki's only home-automation standards body. Spec PDFs sit behind a 403-ing download form; several core specs are directly linkable anyway. (1 source)
- [The Robot Studio](entities/the-robot-studio.md) — open-hardware design group behind **both** the SO-ARM100/101 lineage **and** the HopeJR humanoid arm (confirmed via [LeRobot ICLR 2026 paper](sources/lerobot-iclr-2026-paper.md) Appendix A). (2 sources)
- [AGIBOT](entities/agibot.md) — Shanghai embodied-AI / humanoid company. Open-source-heavy. (8 sources)
- [Hello Robot](entities/hello-robot.md) — Stretch mobile manipulator + stretch_ai stack. (10 sources)
- [Kinova](entities/kinova.md) — Canadian (Quebec) maker of the [Jaco](entities/kinova-jaco.md) assistive arm (2010) + Gen2/Gen3 research manipulators; reimbursement-market business model. (1 source)
- [HCR Lab](entities/hcrlab.md) — Human-Centered Robotics Lab, UW (Maya Cakmak); assistive robots + EUP; Stretch platform; long-term in-home deployments. (10 sources)
- [Quality of Life Technology Center (CMU + Pitt)](entities/cmu-qolt-center.md) — NSF Engineering Research Center (2006–2016, ~$30M; Kanade → Siewiorek, + Cooper); PerMMA two-armed robotic wheelchair + HERB (QoLTbots testbed); "symbiosis over autonomy" doctrine; institutional ancestor of today's PAR field; graduated 2016. (2 sources)
- [Elephant Robotics](entities/elephant-robotics.md) — Chinese edu-robotics vendor; myAGV + myBuddy 280 + arm ecosystem. (2 sources)
- [Pollen Robotics](entities/pollen-robotics.md) — **Bordeaux; founded 2016 by ex-Inria researchers; [Hugging Face](entities/hugging-face.md)'s robotics team since April 2025.** Three products: [Reachy 2](entities/reachy.md) (research manipulator), [Reachy Mini](entities/reachy-mini.md) (desktop HRI, **10,000+ shipped**), [Microduck](entities/microduck.md) (**$399** RL biped). Openness is **per-product and per-layer** — software Apache-2.0 throughout, hardware varies and Microduck is explicitly *not* open-source hardware. (5 sources)
- [Fauna Robotics](entities/fauna-robotics.md) — NYC; Sprout Creator Edition; 107cm, 29 DOF, Jetson AGX Orin. (2 sources)
- [Zeroth Robotics](entities/zeroth-robotics.md) — consumer/light-commercial robot vendor (reported Chinese, linked to Suzhou JoyIn); CES 2026 US launch; M1 $2,499 / W1 $7,999 / Jupiter $89,999, all pre-order. Company pages all 404; funding and AI stack secondary-sourced only. (1 source)
- [K-Scale Labs](entities/k-scale-labs.md) — YC humanoid startup; shut down late 2025; notable post-mortem; was Embodied AI Hackathon mentor + SIGRobotics-UIUC "Mini Humanoid" project sponsor. (3 sources)
- [Welch Labs](entities/welch-labs.md) — independent AI-pedagogy operation (Stephen Welch). YouTube channel + the Feb 2026 *Illustrated Guide to AI* book + GitHub code. One of the wiki's three canonical pedagogy publishers alongside Sutton & Barto and 3Blue1Brown. (3 sources)
- [Meta FAIR](entities/meta-fair.md) — Yann LeCun's lab; JEPA research line. (18 sources)
- [Google DeepMind](entities/google-deepmind.md) — MuJoCo, Newton co-development, MjcPhysics USD plugin, Gemini Robotics. (16 sources)
- [Boston Dynamics](entities/boston-dynamics.md) — robotics company (Hyundai-owned); Spot + Atlas + Stretch + Orbit + AIVI-Learning. (7 sources)
- [Mila](entities/mila.md) — Quebec AI Institute; frequent JEPA collaborator. (4 sources)
- [Farama Foundation](entities/farama-foundation.md) — non-profit; took over OpenAI gym → Gymnasium; 19 RL projects. (3 sources)
- [Dronecode Foundation](entities/dronecode-foundation.md) — non-profit (Linux Foundation Collaborative Project); steward of PX4, MAVLink, Pixhawk, QGroundControl, MAVSDK. (2 sources)
- [AMI Labs](entities/ami-labs.md) — Yann LeCun's post-Meta AI lab; $1.03B seed (TechCrunch-corroborated). Its **first affiliated byline** appears on [WorldDP](entities/worlddp.md) (Jun 2026), updating the earlier "no AMI Labs paper found" note — though it's an NYU co-authorship, not lab-authored work. (7 sources)
- [Logical Intelligence](entities/logical-intelligence.md) — commercializes [energy-based reasoning models](concepts/learning/energy-based-models.md) for critical systems; Eve Bodnia CEO, [Yann LeCun](entities/yann-lecun.md) Founding Chair of Tech Research Board, [Michael Freedman](entities/michael-freedman.md) Chief of Math. Products: [Aleph](entities/aleph.md) (formal-verification agent) + [Kona](entities/kona.md) (EBM reasoning model). Distinct from AMI Labs. (2 sources)
- [Anthropic](entities/anthropic.md) — developer of Claude; AI safety mission; author of Claude's Constitution; MCP protocol; [Frontier Red Team](entities/frontier-red-team.md) robotics uplift work. (12 sources)
- [Anthropic Frontier Red Team](entities/frontier-red-team.md) — Anthropic's capability-evaluation team; runs randomized **[uplift studies](concepts/safety/ai-uplift.md)** (biological risk → robotics) feeding the Responsible Scaling Policy's capability thresholds; author of the three-part **Project Fetch → Phase Two → robotics-evaluation** arc, all on one [Unitree Go2](entities/unitree-go2.md). (3 sources)
- [Apollo Research](entities/apollo-research.md) — independent AI safety evaluation institute; red-teamed Claude Opus 4 (2025). (2 sources)
- [Physical Intelligence](entities/physical-intelligence.md) — San Francisco; π-series (π0 / π0.5 / π0.6 / π0.6-MEM / π0.7 / π*0.6) cross-platform generalist VLAs. (15 sources)
- [Hillbot](entities/hillbot.md) — UCSD spinoff that maintains ManiSkill. (1 source) _stub_
- [Disney Research](entities/disney-research.md) — Newton co-developer with NVIDIA + DeepMind. (2 sources) _stub_
- [FIRST Robotics Competition](entities/first-robotics-competition.md) — world's leading high-school robotics competition; ~3,700 teams, 30+ countries. (7 sources)
- [The Blue Alliance](entities/the-blue-alliance.md) — open-source volunteer FRC data platform (teams / results / match video / GameDay webcasts / public API); the wiki's authoritative third-party FRC results source. (2 sources)
- [AndyMark](entities/andymark.md) — major FRC vendor; AM14U6 chassis, field elements, FUEL scoring elements. (2 sources)
- [Team 254: The Cheesy Poofs](entities/team-254.md) — elite FRC team (2022 World Champions); 2026 "AI in FRC" presentation; Claude Code + wpilib-agent-tools. (3 sources)
- [Team 4414 (HighTide)](entities/team-4414-hightide.md) — Ventura CA FRC team (rookie year 2012); **2026 REBUILT World Champions** (RIPCURRENT, 70–2–0, Rank 1 every event); AI-first dev ("little code written by hand"), state-machine codebase, shot-trajectory precompute. (3 sources)
- [HHMI Janelia Research Campus](entities/hhmi-janelia.md) — HHMI's pure-research lab; Turaga lab leads flybody + flyvis; *Drosophila* neuroscience & connectomics anchor. (3 sources)
- [NeLy-EPFL (Neuroengineering Laboratory)](entities/nely-epfl.md) — EPFL lab; maintains [NeuroMechFly](entities/neuromechfly.md) + the `flygym` Python library; European counterweight to HHMI Janelia in fly-body simulation. (2 sources)
- [Toyota Research Institute (TRI)](entities/tri.md) — Toyota's R&D arm; Los Altos + Cambridge; co-affiliation across [Diffusion Policy](entities/diffusion-policy.md) + [UMI](entities/umi.md); home of TRI [LBM](concepts/learning/large-behavior-models.md) ([Tedrake](entities/russ-tedrake.md)-led; [primary paper ingested](sources/tri-lbm-paper.md)). Its LBM leadership spun out as [Walden Robotics](entities/walden-robotics.md) (2026). (5 sources)

- [Everyday Robots](entities/everyday-robots.md) — Alphabet/X robotics company (wound down 2023); its mobile manipulator is the hardware under **every** real-world number in the pre-2026 code-as-policy lineage. (3 sources)

- [ASIMOV Benchmark](entities/asimov-benchmark.md) — [Google DeepMind](entities/google-deepmind.md)'s [semantic-safety](concepts/safety/semantic-safety.md) benchmark; 5 subsets, injury data from [NEISS](entities/neiss.md), 84.3% top alignment with generated robot constitutions. (6 sources)
- [RoboART](entities/roboart.md) — automated red-teaming pipeline: generative image edits + embedding-space anomaly detection with conformal thresholds, predicting policy degradation without hardware. (2 sources)
- [Veo](entities/veo.md) — DeepMind's video foundation model, specialized for robotics as an action-conditioned, multi-view-consistent **policy-evaluation simulator**. (1 source)
- [NEISS](entities/neiss.md) — US emergency-department injury surveillance (~500k injuries/yr, ~100 hospitals); the empirical backbone of ASIMOV-Injury and the wiki's clearest case of grounding an AI safety benchmark in real harm statistics. (1 source)

### Simulators / frameworks
- [MuJoCo Warp (MJWarp)](entities/mujoco-warp.md) — the **GPU-accelerated [MuJoCo](entities/mujoco.md)**, on NVIDIA Warp. *"Maintained by Google DeepMind and NVIDIA as part of the [Newton](entities/newton-physics-engine.md) project"* — so the DeepMind and NVIDIA simulator lines share this piece. The second GPU attempt after **MJX** (JAX, which [MuJoCo Playground](entities/mujoco-playground.md) uses), and the one the newer stacks adopted: [mjlab](entities/mjlab.md) is *"Isaac Lab API, powered by MuJoCo-Warp."* [Microduck](entities/microduck.md)'s seven shipped policies trace back through it — the wiki's only shipping consumer robot trained on this substrate. No throughput comparison vs MJX or [Isaac Lab](entities/nvidia-isaac-lab.md) ingested. (1 source)
- [mjlab](entities/mjlab.md) — **MuJoCo Warp + `rsl_rl`** RL training framework; Isaac-Lab ergonomics (task registry, env-cfg modules, `train`/`play`/`export`) on the MuJoCo side rather than the PhysX/Newton side. First-class custom actuator classes, MJCF-native, ONNX export with the observation normalizer baked into the graph, and headless submission to Hugging Face Jobs. The stack behind [Microduck](entities/microduck.md)'s seven shipped policies. (1 source)
- [CaP-X](entities/cap-x.md) — open-access framework for benchmarking [code-as-policy](concepts/agents/code-as-policy.md) agents ([GEAR](entities/nvidia-gear.md)/Berkeley, ICML 2026). CaP-Gym (187 tasks over [robosuite](entities/robosuite.md) + [LIBERO-PRO](entities/libero.md) + [BEHAVIOR](entities/behavior-benchmark.md)) + CaP-Bench's 8-tier abstraction ladder + CaP-Agent0 + CaP-RL. The wiki's finest-grained instrument for [control abstraction levels](concepts/robotics/control-abstraction-levels.md). (2 sources)
- [ASPIRE](entities/aspire.md) — continual-learning system that autonomously writes and **repairs** robot programs, compounding validated fixes into a skill library ([GEAR](entities/nvidia-gear.md), Jun 2026). Built on CaP-X. First measured evidence in the wiki that a skill library compounds and transfers cross-embodiment. (1 source)
- [VoxPoser](entities/voxposer.md) — LLM writes code composing **3D value maps** for a motion planner (Stanford, CoRL 2023); 88% real-world vs a primitives baseline's 24%. (2 sources)
- [Language to Rewards](entities/language-to-rewards.md) — LLM writes **reward functions** optimized by MuJoCo MPC (Google DeepMind, CoRL 2023); 90% of 17 tasks vs 50% for Code as Policies. (2 sources)
- [SayCan](entities/saycan.md) — LLM proposes, learned **affordance value functions** dispose (Google/Everyday Robots, CoRL 2022). The skill-selection branch; ~0% under disturbance. (3 sources)
- [Inner Monologue](entities/inner-monologue.md) — closes SayCan's loop with textual environment feedback (CoRL 2022); 30.8% → 60.4% under disturbance. (2 sources)
- [Voyager](entities/voyager.md) — lifelong Minecraft agent with an ever-growing executable-code **skill library** ([GEAR](entities/nvidia-gear.md), TMLR 2024); [ASPIRE](entities/aspire.md)'s direct ancestor, same lab. (3 sources)
- [CodeAct](entities/codeact.md) — code as the general LLM-agent action space (UIUC, ICML 2024); the non-robotics grounding for "code beats a tool schema." (2 sources)
- [MineDojo](entities/minedojo.md) — open-ended Minecraft benchmark ([GEAR](entities/nvidia-gear.md)); the cheap-trial domain Voyager runs on. (2 sources)
- [CLIPort](entities/cliport.md) — language-conditioned imitation policy; the wiki's **collapse baseline**, scoring 0.00 out-of-distribution in 2022 exactly as VLAs do in 2026. (3 sources)
- [robosuite](entities/robosuite.md) — modular MuJoCo-based manipulation framework ([Yuke Zhu](entities/yuke-zhu.md), 2020); the substrate under [LIBERO](entities/libero.md) and one of CaP-Gym's three backends. Infrastructure the wiki had been inheriting silently. (3 sources)
- [Gazebo](entities/gazebo.md) — canonical open-source ROS simulator (Open Robotics); Gazebo Harmonic + ROS 2 Jazzy/Humble; default for [TurtleBot](entities/turtlebot.md), [Stretch](entities/stretch.md), [ROSOrin](entities/rosorin.md), [PX4](entities/px4-autopilot.md); contrast with [Isaac Sim](entities/nvidia-isaac-sim.md) (NVIDIA, higher-fidelity, GPU-required). (7 sources)
- [NVIDIA Isaac Sim](entities/nvidia-isaac-sim.md) — Omniverse-based robotics simulator. (17 sources)
- [NVIDIA Isaac Lab](entities/nvidia-isaac-lab.md) — open-source learning framework on Isaac Sim; primary reference paper is [GEAR](entities/nvidia-gear.md)-authored (arXiv 2511.04831); Lab-Arena environments registrable in LeRobot EnvHub (July 2026). (16 sources)
- [NVIDIA Isaac Teleop](entities/nvidia-isaac-teleop.md) — NVIDIA's teleoperation framework for demo collection (real + sim), leader-arm or XR-headset via CloudXR; `isaacteleop` pip package; LeRobot-native output; launched with [GR00T](entities/nvidia-groot.md) 1.7-in-LeRobot. (4 sources)
- [Newton physics engine](entities/newton-physics-engine.md) — Linux-Foundation, GPU-accelerated. (6 sources)
- [MuJoCo](entities/mujoco.md) — DeepMind-maintained physics engine; substrate for Gymnasium-Robotics, MuJoCo Playground (via MJX), Adroit, Franka Kitchen, DM Control, flybody, NeuroMechFly v2, and Anthropic's [robotics evaluation](sources/anthropic-how-claude-performs-on-robotics-tasks.md). (18 sources)
- [MuJoCo Playground](entities/mujoco-playground.md) — DeepMind's MJX-based learning framework. (5 sources)
- [Genesis](entities/genesis.md) — generative + ultra-fast physics engine. (3 sources)
- [AGIBOT Genie Sim 3.0](entities/agibot-genie-sim.md) — open embodied-AI sim on Isaac Sim. (2 sources)
- [RoboCasa](entities/robocasa.md) — household manipulation benchmark (RoboCasa365 at ICLR 2026); also a GR00T N1 sim benchmark + GR-1 pre-training substrate. (11 sources)
- [ManiSkill](entities/maniskill.md) — [SAPIEN](entities/sapien.md)-based GPU-parallel manipulation benchmark. (4 sources)
- [SAPIEN](entities/sapien.md) — UCSD robot simulation framework underlying ManiSkill. (1 source) _stub_
- [Gymnasium-Robotics](entities/gymnasium-robotics.md) — Farama's [MuJoCo](entities/mujoco.md)-backed robotics envs (Fetch / Hand / Maze / Adroit / Franka Kitchen / MaMuJoCo). (2 sources)
- [Arcade Learning Environment](entities/ale.md) — Farama's Atari 2600 RL benchmark; 100+ single-agent + 23 multi-agent envs; Gymnasium API. (1 source)
- [Unity ML-Agents Toolkit](entities/unity-ml-agents.md) — game-engine RL framework; turns a Unity scene into a training env with built-in PPO / SAC / **MA-POCA** trainers + first-class self-play (ELO) + curriculum; the low-friction [MARL](concepts/learning/multi-agent-rl.md) entry point. (1 source)
- [Metaworld](entities/metaworld.md) — Stanford/Berkeley meta-RL benchmark; 50 manipulation tasks on simulated Sawyer; staple across V-JEPA-line work; natively integrated in LeRobot. (7 sources)
- [RoboArena](entities/roboarena.md) — **distributed real-world pairwise-preference leaderboard** for generalist policies (CoRL 2025). Free task choice + double-blind A/B + extended Bradley-Terry; **r≈0.95 vs oracle where conventional eval gets ≈0.60**, converging in ~100 comparisons. Runs on [DROID](entities/droid.md); the leaderboard [Cosmos 3](entities/nvidia-cosmos.md) tops. Gives ordering, not magnitude. (3 sources)
- [RoboLab](entities/nvidia-robolab.md) — NVIDIA SRL's robot-/policy-agnostic **sim benchmark** for real-world task-generalist policies; language-generated scenes/tasks (saturation-resistant); **RoboLab-120** (120 tasks, 68.7% DROID vocab overlap); the bench [Cosmos 3](entities/nvidia-cosmos.md) policies score on. Now also carries the **[evaluation methodology](concepts/robotics/robot-policy-evaluation.md)**: Clopper-Pearson ±2 pp ⇒ ≈1,030 rollouts (~15× typical), SPARC, NPE, competency tags, and the finding that **no policy exceeded ~4 complex subtasks**. Folding into [Isaac Lab-Arena](entities/nvidia-isaac-lab.md) Aug 2026. RSS 2026. (2 sources)
- [PushT](entities/pusht.md) — 2D T-block pushing benchmark; introduced by IBC, popularized by Diffusion Policy; default lightweight bench across LeWM / DINO-WM / JEPA-WMs. (8 sources)
- [PointMaze](entities/pointmaze.md) — 2D point-mass maze navigation; default lightweight nav bench across LeWM / DINO-WM / JEPA-WMs. (2 sources)
- [DM Control Suite](entities/dm-control.md) — DeepMind continuous-control RL benchmark on top of MuJoCo; pre-Gymnasium-Robotics legacy substrate. (3 sources)
- [WorldArena](entities/worldarena.md) — the **embodied world-model benchmark**: perception (16 metrics → EWMScore) *plus* functional utility as data engine / policy evaluator / action planner; 2.0 adds visuotactile, RL environments, and a real ALOHA. Source of the wiki's **perception–functionality gap** numbers and of "learned dynamics can shape a policy, not be one." Built on [RoboTwin 2.0](entities/robotwin.md). (3 sources)
- [WorldRoamBench](entities/worldroambench.md) — long-horizon stability of **interactive** world models (10–60 s WASD roaming, 600+ cases, 4 dimensions incl. action-decoupled **memory** via 3D point-cloud reconstruction). The rigorous form of the brief's "leave the scene and return" probe. Ranks [Genie 3](entities/genie-3.md) 1st in FPV. (1 source)
- [Ctrl-World](entities/ctrl-world.md) — [Chelsea Finn](entities/chelsea-finn.md)-group action-conditioned world model; **best policy evaluator measured** (r = 0.986 vs simulator ranking), best long-horizon RL environment, 2nd on EWMScore. The wiki's strongest evidence that **action conditioning is load-bearing**. Primary paper not yet ingested. (2 sources) _stub_
- [LIBERO](entities/libero.md) — lifelong-learning manipulation benchmark (Liu et al. NeurIPS 2023); de-facto VLA-eval bench (SPATIAL / OBJECT / GOAL / 90 / LONG task families); natively integrated in LeRobot; **top *tier*: [X-VLA](entities/x-vla.md) 98.1, [MolmoAct2](entities/molmoact2.md) 97.2 / Think 98.1, [TurboVLA](entities/turbovla.md) 97.7, OpenVLA-OFT 97.1, GR00T 1.7 96.5 — a ten-model [statistical tie](syntheses/platforms/vla-success-rate-audit.md) spanning 1.2 pp**. Protocol confirmed: **50 episodes/task → 500/suite**. ⚠️ [LIBERO-PRO](sources/libero-pro-paper.md): >90% here → **0.0% under perturbation** — the benchmark may be measuring memorization. Also repurposed by Anthropic as a **direct-LLM-control** benchmark: general frontier models score **0–5.5%** end-to-end where trained VLAs score ~97% — the wiki's clearest quantification of what action-pretraining buys. (19 sources)
- [RoboTwin 2.0](entities/robotwin.md) — bimanual **data generator** + benchmark; 50 tasks × 5 embodiments, **100k+ pre-collected trajectories**, RoboTwin-OD (731 objects). **Primary source now ingested.** Protocol confirmed at **5,000 rollouts/model/condition** — the better instrument versus [LIBERO](entities/libero.md)'s ten-model tie. Its grasp-adaptation result (**Piper 2.4% → 25.1%**, Franka −0.1) is the wiki's sharpest evidence that low-DoF arms are under-served at the data layer. (5 sources)
- [SimplerEnv](entities/simplerenv.md) — Sapien-adjacent mid-weight sim positioned as real-world-correlation harness; used by VLA-JEPA. (3 sources)
- [Habitat](entities/habitat.md) — Meta FAIR embodied-AI sim (navigation + manipulation in photorealistic 3D scenes); legacy substrate. (1 source)
- [BEHAVIOR / BEHAVIOR-1K](entities/behavior-benchmark.md) — Stanford's 1,000-task household-activity benchmark ([paper](sources/behavior-1k-paper.md); survey-grounded, BDDL, 9,000+ objects); the **hard end of the sim-to-real gap** (12.4% real-household vs 89.4% RLBench); a [GR00T N1.6](sources/groot-n1_6.md) sim substrate. (6 sources)
