- [SERL](entities/serl.md) — Sample-Efficient Robotic RL suite (Luo et al. 2024); RLPD + reward classifier + forward-backward auto-reset + impedance control; 25–50 min/policy; HIL-SERL's predecessor. (5 sources)

### LLMs
- [Nemotron](entities/nemotron.md) — NVIDIA's open-weights family, and **the model bundled into NVIDIA's own agent scaffolding** ([NemoClaw](entities/nemoclaw.md)). **3.5 Lightning** is a 30B MoE for always-on agents (vLLM/Ollama/llama.cpp/LM Studio, NVFP4 + GGUF, RTX → DGX Spark → **Jetson**); **3.5 ASR** runs [Portable Computer](entities/perplexity-portable-computer.md)'s on-device dictation. No model card ingested — the 4×/30% speed claims name no baseline. (2 sources)
- [Qwen](entities/qwen.md) — Alibaba's open-weights LLM family. Default local LLM in both stretch_ai (3B) and ROSOrin (1.7B). (7 sources)

### Tools
- [Asta](entities/asta.md) — [Ai2](entities/ai2.md)'s open **agentic ecosystem for scientific discovery** (AI-for-Science pillar): Asta Agents + AstaBench leaderboards + Asta Resources; AutoDiscovery for autonomous hypothesis generation (Bayesian surprise). Live at asta.allen.ai. (1 source)
- [OlmoEarth](entities/olmoearth.md) — Ai2's open **earth-observation foundation model** (AI-for-the-Planet pillar); the "Olmo" brand extended to geospatial; open models + open pretraining data; ~97% wetland-detection beta; underlies **Skylight** (ocean) + **EarthRanger** (wildlife). (1 source)
- [Ollama](entities/ollama.md) — local LLM runtime (used by ROSOrin offline curriculum). (6 sources)
- [MimicGen](entities/mimicgen.md) — synthetic-demo expansion tool used by RoboCasa365; **DexMimicGen** variant multiplies demos ~780k-trajectory-scale for [GR00T N1](sources/groot-n1-paper.md). (5 sources)
- [NVIDIA Brev](entities/nvidia-brev.md) — NVIDIA's cross-cloud GPU-instance broker (`brev` CLI + Launchables); no native auto-stop, so cost discipline is on the user. (4 sources)
- [jetson-containers](entities/jetson-containers.md) — Dustin Franklin / NVIDIA framework for building + running Docker images on Jetson (aarch64/L4T); `dustynv/*` image registry + `autotag`/`run.sh` helpers; the deployment substrate behind Jetson AI Lab tutorials (incl. `dustynv/lerobot`). The edge-side counterpart to Brev. (3 sources)
- [jetson-examples (reComputer runner)](entities/jetson-examples.md) — Seeed's MIT `reComputer run <example>` CLI atop [jetson-containers](entities/jetson-containers.md); ~37 one-command Jetson AI recipes (LLM/VLM/vision/gen-AI/robotics); `pip3 install jetson-examples`; $250 contribution bounty. (4 sources)
- [Ultralytics YOLO](entities/ultralytics-yolo.md) — de-facto real-time CV library + model family (detect/segment/classify/pose/OBB/track); flagship YOLO26; `pip install ultralytics`; ONNX/TensorRT edge export; AGPL-3.0/Enterprise. (11 sources)
- [RT-DETR](entities/rt-detr.md) — Baidu's **transformer** (DETR-lineage) real-time detector; **NMS-free by set prediction**; the standing non-YOLO benchmark foil across the wiki's detection thread (RT-DETR-R18 46.5 AP; YOLOv10-S ~1.8× faster at parity). (4 sources)
- [YOLO-NAS](entities/yolo-nas.md) — Deci's **NAS-designed** (AutoNAC) YOLO variant optimized for **INT8 quantization robustness** / edge deployment; no formal paper (blog+repo release); Deci non-commercial weights license. (2 sources)
- [DEIM](entities/deim.md) — **DETR with Improved Matching** (Dense O2O + MAL); fixes DETR's slow convergence on the [RT-DETR](entities/rt-detr.md) line; a YOLO26 transformer benchmark cross-comparison. (1 source)
- [NeMo Guardrails](entities/nemo-guardrails.md) — [NVIDIA](entities/nvidia.md)'s open-source runtime guardrail toolkit: five rail types (input/retrieval/dialog/**execution**/output) in **Colang** + YAML, the NemoGuard NIMs, a pluggable third-party guard market, PyPI-or-microservice deployment, and an **OpenAI-compatible guardrails server**. Ships the tool-call *hook*, not the *policy*. (3 sources)
- [garak](entities/garak.md) — open-source **LLM vulnerability scanner** ("nmap for LLMs"; NVIDIA). The build-phase counterpart to Guardrails' run-phase job; productized as NeMo Auditor (2026-04). Probes text endpoints only — no notion of a robot's tool-call channel. (2 sources)
- [ZeroMQ](entities/zeromq.md) — brokerless embeddable messaging library (sockets + patterns over inproc/IPC/TCP/UDP/WebSocket); the transport under [Isaac-GR00T](sources/isaac-gr00t-github.md)'s policy server/client; completes the wiki's transport map (DDS ↔ ROS 2, gRPC ↔ LeRobot async, ZMQ ↔ GR00T). (2 sources)

### Reasoning / formal-verification models
- [Aleph](entities/aleph.md) — [Logical Intelligence](entities/logical-intelligence.md)'s agentic orchestration product; pairs frontier LLM (GPT-5.2) + [Kona](entities/kona.md) + [Lean](concepts/learning/lean-theorem-prover.md); **99.4% / 668-of-672 on [PutnamBench](concepts/learning/putnambench.md)** (May 2026). (2 sources)
- [Kona](entities/kona.md) — non-autoregressive [energy-based reasoning model](concepts/learning/energy-based-models.md) from [Logical Intelligence](entities/logical-intelligence.md); 16M–200M params; pilots in energy / advanced manufacturing / semiconductor (Q1 2026). (4 sources)

### Events
- [LeRobot Worldwide Hackathon 2025](entities/lerobot-worldwide-hackathon-2025.md) — Hugging Face hybrid hackathon, June 14–15, 2025; 916 team members, ~400 submissions, 30 ranked winners; prizes: Hope Jr Arm / LeKiwi / SO-101. (1 source)

### People
- [Marco Pavone](entities/marco-pavone.md) — Stanford (MechE + AeroAstro), Autonomous Systems Lab. Senior author on the wiki's [OSCBF](sources/oscbf-paper.md) and [SD-LQR](sources/learning-control-oriented-dynamical-structure.md) sources; the through-line is **making learned components admissible to classical control machinery** — give a learned model the structure a controller needs, or wrap a learned policy in a filter with an invariance proof, measure what the wrapping costs and fix it ([PACS](sources/pacs-paper.md)), and detect when the policy is failing anyway ([Sentinel](sources/sentinel-paper.md)). (5 sources)
- [Adithyavairavan Murali](entities/adithyavairavan-murali.md) — [NVIDIA](entities/nvidia.md); senior author on [GraspGen-X](sources/graspgenx-paper.md) and first author of GraspGen (2025) and the 2020 grasping-in-clutter work that introduced the pose-retargeting heuristic GraspGen-X measures and beats. The through-line is **grasping as a reusable module** handed to a planner, not as behaviour absorbed into a policy. (1 source)
- [Nick Maselli](entities/nick-maselli.md) — founder of [Vulcan Robotics](entities/vulcan-robotics.md) ([Sourccey](entities/sourccey.md)). (1 source) _stub_
- [Michele Colledanchise](entities/michele-colledanchise.md) / [Petter Ögren](entities/petter-ogren.md) — authors of the [behavior-trees book](sources/behavior-trees-book.md); Ögren's formal analysis is the part most relevant to robot guardrails. (1 source each) _stubs_
- [Tom Silver](entities/tom-silver.md) — symbolic planning / TAMP; first author of the [PDDL generalized-planning paper](sources/generalized-planning-pddl-llm-paper.md) and its No-Names ablation. (1 source) _stub_
- [Tobia Marcucci](entities/tobia-marcucci.md) — originator of the **[Graphs of Convex Sets](concepts/robotics/graphs-of-convex-sets.md)** framework and first author of the [GCS motion planner](sources/gcs-motion-planning-paper.md). The mathematical object is his; the planner is [Tedrake](entities/russ-tedrake.md)'s group's — a split the framework paper's co-author list (Parrilo; SIAM J. Optimization) confirms. (3 sources)
- [Josh Tenenbaum](entities/josh-tenenbaum.md) / [Leslie Pack Kaelbling](entities/leslie-kaelbling.md) — MIT; co-authors on the same paper. Kaelbling founded the **TAMP** line that bridges symbolic specs to motion — **uncovered in this wiki**. (1 source each) _stubs_
- [Dorsa Sadigh](entities/dorsa-sadigh.md) — Stanford; HRI + interactive imitation learning; senior author on [RT-H](entities/rt-h.md), whose 50-pp language-vs-teleop correction result is an HRI finding in a VLA's clothes. (1 source) _stub_
- [Suneel Belkhale](entities/suneel-belkhale.md) — Stanford/DeepMind; first author of [RT-H](entities/rt-h.md). (1 source) _stub_
- [Ted Xiao](entities/ted-xiao.md) — [Google DeepMind](entities/google-deepmind.md) robotics; co-author across the RT-1/RT-2/RT-H line. (1 source) _stub_
- [Xiang Bai](entities/xiang-bai.md) — [HUST](entities/hust.md) professor; senior author on [TurboVLA](entities/turbovla.md). (1 source) _stub_
- [Dingkang Liang](entities/dingkang-liang.md) — [HUST](entities/hust.md); project lead on [TurboVLA](entities/turbovla.md), and an author of the LLM-centric DDVLA it supersedes on its own table. (1 source) _stub_
- [Yann LeCun](entities/yann-lecun.md) — NYU; Turing Award 2018; architect of the JEPA program; founder of [AMI Labs](entities/ami-labs.md) (whose first affiliated byline appears on [WorldDP](entities/worlddp.md), Jun 2026). Nine papers here carry his name. Co-authored the original 1993 Siamese network paper at AT&T Bell Labs. (29 sources)
- [Tomas Mikolov](entities/tomas-mikolov.md) — then Google, previously Brno. **The author of word2vec** ([2013a](sources/mikolov2013-efficient-estimation-word-representations.md), [2013b](sources/mikolov2013-distributed-representations-words-phrases.md)); turned learned embeddings from a component inside a language model into a downloadable artifact. Consistent bet: a worse model on far more data beats a better model on less — and he published the result that his own prior RNNLM line was solving the wrong problem. (2 sources)
- [Ilya Sutskever](entities/ilya-sutskever.md) — Google Brain → OpenAI co-founder/Chief Scientist → SSI; Hinton student and AlexNet co-author. Here for two consecutive papers: [seq2seq](sources/sutskever2014-sequence-to-sequence-learning.md) (first author — the encoder–decoder attention then replaced) and [word2vec paper 2](sources/mikolov2013-distributed-representations-words-phrases.md) (second author — negative sampling). (2 sources)
- [Dzmitry Bahdanau](entities/dzmitry-bahdanau.md) — first author of [the attention paper](sources/bahdanau2014-neural-machine-translation-align-translate.md), and **fourth author of [the encoder–decoder paper](sources/cho2014-rnn-encoder-decoder-phrase-representations.md) whose bottleneck he removed three months later**. Per his own account, the mechanism came from introspecting on middle-school translation homework — gaze shifting between source and target — and **worked on the first try**. (1 source)
- [Kyunghyun Cho](entities/kyunghyun-cho.md) — Montréal → NYU; first author of [the paper that introduced the **GRU** and the RNN Encoder–Decoder](sources/cho2014-rnn-encoder-decoder-phrase-representations.md), and second author of [the attention paper](sources/bahdanau2014-neural-machine-translation-align-translate.md) that beats his own baseline by 7.6 BLEU three months later. Named neither of his own mechanisms — the GRU is "a hidden unit that adaptively remembers and forgets," and attention was *RNNsearch*. (2 sources)
- [Jeff Dean](entities/jeff-dean.md) — Google Senior Fellow; the **infrastructure** half of the [word2vec](sources/mikolov2013-efficient-estimation-word-representations.md) papers. DistBelief is why a deletion-shaped architectural contribution had a scale at which to pay off — the same asynchronous-parameter-server idea [Bengio et al. 2003](sources/bengio2003-neural-probabilistic-language-model.md) improvised on 40 CPUs, a decade later as production infrastructure. (2 sources)
- [Yoshua Bengio](entities/yoshua-bengio.md) — Université de Montréal / Mila; Turing Award 2018. In this wiki, the origin of the **learned embedding table**: first author of [A Neural Probabilistic Language Model](sources/bengio2003-neural-probabilistic-language-model.md) (JMLR 2003), whose §5.1 energy variant is also the wiki's earliest [EBM](concepts/learning/energy-based-models.md) — a Bengio construction, not a LeCun one. His attention-for-NMT work (Bahdanau/Cho/Bengio 2014) is the notable gap. (1 source)
- [Geoffrey Hinton](entities/geoffrey-hinton.md) — Toronto; Turing Award 2018, Nobel 2024; first author of [wake-sleep](sources/wake-sleep-paper.md) / Helmholtz machine — the recognition-model ancestor of the VAE encoder; RMSprop, DBNs, backprop popularization. Also the cited origin of **distributed representations** (1986) and of the products-of-experts framing behind the [NPLM](sources/bengio2003-neural-probabilistic-language-model.md)'s energy variant. (4 sources)
- [Moo Jin Kim](entities/moo-jin-kim.md) — Stanford; first author of [OpenVLA](entities/openvla.md) and [OpenVLA-OFT](entities/openvla-oft.md), co-author on [RoboArena](entities/roboarena.md). His arc runs building → controlled ablation → evaluation protocol. (3 sources)
- [Wenlong Huang](entities/wenlong-huang.md) — Stanford/Google; on three of the four foundational code-as-policy papers (first author of [VoxPoser](entities/voxposer.md), co-first of [Inner Monologue](entities/inner-monologue.md)). His VoxPoser critiques the Code as Policies he co-authored. (3 sources)
- [Brian Ichter](entities/brian-ichter.md) — Google → co-founder, [Physical Intelligence](entities/physical-intelligence.md). On **all four** Google-lineage papers, then helped found the [π0](entities/pi-zero.md) lab — one person on both sides of the wiki's central architectural argument. (4 sources)
- [Karol Hausman](entities/karol-hausman.md) — SayCan corresponding author → CEO of [Physical Intelligence](entities/physical-intelligence.md). Same crossing as Ichter. (3 sources)
- [Andy Zeng](entities/andy-zeng.md) — senior author of [Code as Policies](sources/code-as-policies-paper.md); also authored Transporter Nets, the architecture under [CLIPort](entities/cliport.md), the baseline his later papers show collapsing. (4 sources)
- [Fei Xia](entities/fei-xia.md) — Google DeepMind; SayCan corresponding author, Inner Monologue co-first, on all four. (4 sources)
- [Guanzhi Wang](entities/guanzhi-wang.md) — [GEAR](entities/nvidia-gear.md)/Caltech; **the direct bridge across the thread's two halves** — first author of [Voyager](entities/voyager.md), co-author of [CaP-X](entities/cap-x.md), project co-lead on [ASPIRE](entities/aspire.md). (3 sources)
- [Fei-Fei Li](entities/fei-fei-li.md) — Stanford; senior author on [VoxPoser](entities/voxposer.md) and co-author on [CaP-X](entities/cap-x.md), bracketing the thread. Her group also built [BEHAVIOR](entities/behavior-benchmark.md), one of CaP-Gym's backends. Also founding director of [Stanford HAI](entities/stanford-hai.md) and CEO of [World Labs](entities/world-labs.md); co-author of the 2026 [world-model policy brief](sources/hai-world-model-spatial-intelligence-brief.md) **and** of the [taxonomy essay](sources/world-labs-functional-taxonomy.md) it borrows its framework from. The dual role sharpened in July 2026: the brief recommends public investment in shared simulation infrastructure, and four weeks later her company [bought its way into simulation](sources/world-labs-scenix-acquisition.md). (6 sources)
- [Yunzhu Li](entities/yunzhu-li.md) — **co-founder of [SceniX](entities/scenix.md)**; Assistant Professor of CS at Columbia; PhD MIT, postdoc at Stanford with [Fei-Fei Li](entities/fei-fei-li.md). Co-author on [VoxPoser](sources/voxposer-paper.md) and [CodeAct](sources/codeact-paper.md). North star: *"make robots work. In the real environment."* Positions worth having: simulation and real data **don't contradict**; judge fidelity by *essential structure* not realism; **semi-structured before unstructured**; robots get **no human-in-the-loop grace period** the way LLMs do; video world models fail at **object permanence under contact**. (4 sources)
- [Changxi Zheng](entities/changxi-zheng.md) — [SceniX](entities/scenix.md) co-founder; Columbia CS professor in **physical simulation and graphics**, VFX background (Weta, Tencent). The simulation half of the founding pair — R2S2R's appearance-*and*-dynamics claim in personnel form. (1 source)
- [Vikas Sindhwani](entities/vikas-sindhwani.md) — [Google DeepMind](entities/google-deepmind.md); **on all four papers** of the robot-safety cluster, from conic-optimization guarantees to prompt-loaded robot constitutions. What makes it one program rather than four papers. (6 sources)
- [Pierre Sermanet](entities/pierre-sermanet.md) — DeepMind; first author of [ASIMOV](entities/asimov-benchmark.md), also on [Inner Monologue](sources/inner-monologue-paper.md). Arc runs from the code-as-policy lineage into what a robot should *refuse*. (5 sources)
- [Anirudha Majumdar](entities/anirudha-majumdar.md) — Princeton/DeepMind; first author of [Predictive Red Teaming](entities/roboart.md). Brings conformal prediction — distribution-free uncertainty quantification — to robot policies. (4 sources)
- [Amir Ali Ahmadi](entities/amir-ali-ahmadi.md) — Princeton ORFE; SDP/polynomial optimization for control. Anchors the formal pole of the safety cluster. (1 source)
- [Ken Goldberg](entities/ken-goldberg.md) — UC Berkeley, directs the AUTOLab; co-senior author (with [Jim Fan](entities/jim-fan.md)) on **both** ingested [code-as-policy](concepts/agents/code-as-policy.md) papers. The Berkeley grasping-and-manipulation framing is why that line reasons in composable classical primitives. (2 sources)
- [Letian (Max) Fu](entities/letian-fu.md) — UC Berkeley/[NVIDIA](entities/nvidia.md); lead author of [CaP-X](entities/cap-x.md) and co-author of [ASPIRE](entities/aspire.md) — both papers [Waddle](entities/waddle-labs.md) named as its closest prior work. Wrote the measurement apparatus, then helped build the system that beats it. (2 sources)
- [Douglas Hofstadter](entities/douglas-hofstadter.md) — cognitive scientist; *GEB* (1979) and *I Am a Strange Loop* (2007). Origin of the **strange loop** both 2025–26 self-reference sources build on. Cited authority, not an ingested author — neither primary text is in the wiki. (2 sources)
- [Andrej Karpathy](entities/andrej-karpathy.md) — independent AI researcher/educator; formerly Tesla AI director + OpenAI founding member; author of micrograd / nanoGPT / nanochat / autoresearch and the [Zero to Hero](sources/karpathy-nn-zero-to-hero.md) lecture series — the wiki's recommended pedagogical references for backprop, transformers, end-to-end LLM training, and agent-driven ML research. (6 sources)
- [Kevin Clark](entities/kevin-clark.md) — Stanford NLP PhD (Manning group); ELECTRA lead author; wrote the CS224n [vectorized-backprop gradient notes](sources/clark-computing-nn-gradients.md). (1 source)
- [Navid Azizan](entities/navid-azizan.md) — MIT ME / IDSS / LIDS; learning-based control; SD-LQR (ICML 2023) + drone adaptive control (2025). (3 sources)
- [Adrien Bardes](entities/adrien-bardes.md) — FAIR researcher; lead author on VICReg (ICLR 2022); co-senior on V-JEPA 2, V-JEPA 2.1, JEPA-WMs. The FAIR-side champion of the V-JEPA program. (6 sources)
- [Randall Balestriero](entities/randall-balestriero.md) — Brown; the **theory-side counterpart to LeCun** in the JEPA program. LeJEPA co-first author (SIGReg), co-author on both May 2026 world-model papers + [SMWM](entities/smwm.md) (with Schölkopf, Jun 2026), and the lab the `stable-worldmodel` repo sits in. Proved the identifiability guarantee *and* co-authored the benchmark showing current models collapse — five days apart. (7 sources)
- [Lucas Maes](entities/lucas-maes.md) — Mila/UdeM; lead author of **both** [LeWorldModel](entities/leworldmodel.md) and [stable-worldmodel](entities/stable-worldmodel.md) — i.e. he published the benchmark that most sharply limits his own model's claims. (3 sources)
- [David Klindt](entities/david-klindt.md) — Cold Spring Harbor; lead author of the identifiability paper. Imports identifiability theory (nonlinear ICA / causal representation learning) into the JEPA program. Thin entity — one source. (1 source)
- [Basile Terver](entities/basile-terver.md) — researcher (FAIR-affiliated, inferred); bread-crumb across DINO-world → JEPA-WMs lineage. (4 sources)
- [Sergey Levine](entities/sergey-levine.md) — UC Berkeley EECS; senior across the real-world-RL lineage (SAC ×2 + RLPD + SERL + HIL-SERL) + DROID + Metaworld. (5 direct-citing source pages)

## Concepts

### Learning
- [Reward post-training of diffusion and flow models](concepts/learning/reward-post-training-diffusion.md) — improving a multi-step generative policy against a reward observed **only at the endpoint**. The four reward-to-target paradigms (trajectory credit / direct reward backprop / endpoint supervision / on-policy self-distillation) separated by *where the desired intermediate change becomes explicit*; the **`G_realized = G_construct − G_fit`** decomposition and the finding that the two stages do not track each other; **step distillation breaking endpoint supervision**, which is where few-step robot action heads sit; and reward-model over-optimization as the standing hazard — **a reward-specific checkpoint scored on its own training reward is not a held-out measurement.** One image-only source; the robotics case is prospective. (1 source)
- [Crowdsourced robot training data](concepts/learning/crowdsourced-robot-training-data.md) — sourcing physical-task data from the public rather than lab staff or teleoperators. Why: supervised corpora top out in the hundreds-to-low-thousands of hours ([DROID](entities/droid.md) 350, [EgoDex](entities/egodex.md) 829, [EgoScale](sources/egoscale-paper.md) 20,854) and [the curve](concepts/learning/scaling-laws-vla.md) has not saturated. What breaks, and is specific to crowdsourcing: **the data becomes adversarial** (paid per accepted clip → optimise acceptance, so audit at the *user* level not the clip level); **an embedding model becomes the arbiter of novelty**, and visual dedup can discard exactly the contact-dynamics variation manipulation needs; **rate is not size and size is not yield**; a marginal-novelty rate quoted without a trend is unfalsifiable; and the consent surface covers everyone in frame, not just the contributor. Solves none of the human→robot label problem. One instance ([Index](entities/figure-index.md)), no published results. (1 source)
- [Actuator fidelity in sim-to-real](concepts/learning/actuator-fidelity-sim2real.md) — the **actuator-side** branch of the reality gap: model the motor's voltage control law, back-EMF, Stribeck friction and gear backlash instead of an ideal PD, then randomize over *battery voltage, voltage sag, command delay and friction* rather than over textures. The claim, from [Microduck](entities/microduck.md): below some mass/cost threshold the actuator **is** the gap, so randomizing an idealised model harder widens the distribution without moving it onto reality. Includes the subtlety that decides whether it works — the real encoder sits on the **output** side of the backlash, so observations must read through the play. One worked example, no independent replication. (1 source)
- [Generative data augmentation for robot learning](concepts/learning/generative-data-augmentation.md) — the three families, separated by **where the action labels come from**: trajectory synthesis in sim ([MimicGen](entities/mimicgen.md), [RoboTwin 2.0](entities/robotwin.md) — exact labels), neural trajectory generation ([DreamGen](entities/dreamgen.md) — IDM pseudo-labels), and **appearance transfer with preserved motion** ([Cosmos](entities/nvidia-cosmos.md) video2video — labels reused verbatim, zero relabeling). Family 3 is the cheapest and the only single-workstation-runnable one, and can *only* fix appearance overfitting — never behavioral diversity. Family 1 is measured (+24.4 pts); **family 3 is not measured anywhere in this wiki.** (6 sources)
- [Soft-prompt cross-embodiment conditioning](concepts/learning/soft-prompt-cross-embodiment.md) — per-data-source learnable embeddings injected early, absorbing camera rig / control rate / task distribution as well as action space. Beats per-embodiment action heads, HPT-style input projection, and hand-written language prompts. **0.04% of params, and the prompts encode configuration similarity rather than dataset identity** (the two DROID viewpoint prompts intermingle). Untested on a **kinematically deficient** target — which [Sourccey](entities/sourccey.md) is about to be. (1 source)
- [Latent action tokens](concepts/learning/latent-action-tokens.md) — discrete codebook indices as a **cross-embodiment** action interface ([UniT](entities/unit.md), [UniVLA](entities/univla.md)); unreadable by construction, and the reason portability and readability keep failing to co-occur. (1 source)
- [Neural geometry](concepts/safety/neural-geometry.md) — the **shape** of a concept's representation, against the default assumption that concepts are *directions*. The **linear representation hypothesis** underlies all of SAE-based interpretability; [Engels et al. (ICLR 2025)](sources/engels2024-not-all-features-one-dimensionally-linear.md) show it is **not universally true** — circular day/month features are irreducibly 2-D, and patching just that subspace matches patching the whole layer. Three consequences: steering breaks in a *diagnosable* way (teleportation artifacts), SAE features become a linear approximation to a curved object, and it supplies a candidate account of Olah's "dark matter" — concepts missing not because we lacked scale but because they aren't shaped like directions. **Prevalence unknown; nothing in robotics.** (4 sources)
- [Inductive bias](concepts/learning/inductive-bias.md) — the assumptions a learner uses to **generalize beyond its training data**. Infinitely many functions fit any finite dataset, so a learner with none cannot generalize, only memorize — **the question is never whether but which and how much**. Lives in five places, not one: architecture, objective, regularizer, optimizer, and data representation (an augmentation set is an *invariance claim*). Bias substitutes for data, so its optimal strength falls as data grows — measured directly by [TDV](sources/tdv-paper.md) (best masking ratio 50% at 0.1% of ImageNet, 30% overtaking it as data grows) and architecturally by [ViT](sources/vit-paper.md) (loses at 1.3M images, wins at 303M while ResNets plateau). Two cautions: **"less inductive bias" nearly always means *relocated*, not removed** — ViT needs positional encoding bolted back on, TDV still needs an EMA teacher and a DINO loss; and an under-constrained bias fails **silently**, which is what [representation collapse](concepts/world-models/jepa.md) is ([Sobal 2022](sources/sobal2022-jepa-slow-features-paper.md): the slowest feature wins even when it isn't the useful one). Closes with the claim that **this cuts the other way in robotics** — the bias-discarding results were earned at data scales robot corpora sit 3–5 orders of magnitude below. (7 sources)
- [Distributed representations (learned embeddings)](concepts/learning/distributed-representations.md) — the `|V| × m` lookup table trained jointly with the task that consumes it, and **why discrete inputs need one**: for continuous variables smoothness gives generalization for free, for discrete alphabets nothing transfers, because one-hot makes `cat` and `dog` exactly as far apart as `cat` and `the`. The fix is not to generalize better over the discrete space but to **manufacture a continuous one**. Two load-bearing properties: the similarity metric is *learned, not specified*, and joint training is not optional — [Bengio et al.](sources/bengio2003-neural-probabilistic-language-model.md) tried fixed LSI-style features and report it failing. Every token embedding, action codebook, [latent action token](concepts/learning/latent-action-tokens.md) and [soft prompt](concepts/learning/soft-prompt-cross-embodiment.md) in this wiki is the same object with a different alphabet. (1 source)
- [VLA models](concepts/learning/vla-models.md) — vision-language-action robot foundation models; four action-head families incl. VLA-0's head-free "action-as-text" + MolmoAct2's hybrid discrete+continuous head. (81 sources)
- [LLM-free VLA (V+L→A)](concepts/learning/llm-free-vla.md) — the **orthogonal axis** to the action-head taxonomy: whether a language model is in the control loop at all. [TurboVLA](entities/turbovla.md) says no and holds the LIBERO tier at 0.2 B. **Language semantics are load-bearing; language models may not be.** Decided by [LIBERO-PRO](sources/libero-pro-paper.md), which nobody has run on it. (1 source)
- [Mixture-of-Experts (MoE)](concepts/learning/mixture-of-experts.md) — many expert FFNs + a router → large *total* params, small *active* per token; the cost-performance lever in big LMs. [OLMoE](entities/olmoe.md) is the open worked example (granular experts, dropless routing, shared-experts-ineffective — contested at frontier scale). (1 source)
- [Large behavior models (LBMs)](concepts/learning/large-behavior-models.md) — TRI's superclass term: any image-sequences→actions model; VLA = the uptrained-VLM subtype, video/world-model backbone = the alternative; "multitask diffusion policy" lineage; [primary paper](sources/tri-lbm-paper.md) ingested (3–5× fine-tune data efficiency; 20–30-pt CIs at 50 rollouts). (4 sources)
- [Imitation learning](concepts/learning/imitation-learning.md) — supervised learning from demonstrations. (65 sources)
- [Real-world robotic RL](concepts/learning/real-world-robot-rl.md) — training RL directly on hardware; the [SAC](entities/sac.md)→[RLPD](entities/rlpd.md)→[SERL](entities/serl.md)→[HIL-SERL](sources/hil-serl-paper.md)→[AutoSERL](sources/autoserl-paper.md) lineage (off-policy + demos + human/automated corrections + sparse classifier reward). (7 sources)
- [Multi-agent reinforcement learning (MARL)](concepts/learning/multi-agent-rl.md) — RL with several simultaneously-learning agents; Markov Games, non-stationarity, independent learning vs. **CTDE** ([MADDPG](glossary.md#maddpg) / MA-POCA), self-play + ELO, cooperative/competitive/mixed. (1 source)
- [Flow matching](concepts/learning/flow-matching.md) — learn a vector field that transports noise to actions; the dominant continuous-action-head technique in 2025+ VLAs ([π0](entities/pi-zero.md), [π0.7](entities/pi07.md), [π*0.6](entities/pistar06.md), [SmolVLA](entities/smolvla.md), [GR00T N1](sources/groot-n1-paper.md), [MolmoAct2](entities/molmoact2.md), [EgoScale](sources/egoscale-paper.md)). Sibling-not-subclass of [DDPM](entities/ddpm.md). (16 sources)
- [Knowledge Insulation (KI)](concepts/learning/knowledge-insulation.md) — [Physical Intelligence](entities/physical-intelligence.md) VLA training recipe ([Driess et al. 2505.23705](sources/knowledge-insulation-paper.md), **primary now ingested**): joint discrete/continuous training + VLM co-training + **stop-gradient** the flow-matching action expert, so the action head can't corrupt the backbone. π0 needs ~7.5× more steps without it. The recipe behind [π0.7](entities/pi07.md) / [π*0.6](entities/pistar06.md); also adopted (then dropped in fine-tuning) by [MolmoAct2](entities/molmoact2.md). (6 sources)
- [Per-layer KV conditioning](concepts/learning/per-layer-kv-conditioning.md) — [MolmoAct2](entities/molmoact2.md)'s VLM→action-expert interface: each flow-matching expert layer cross-attends to the *corresponding* VLM layer's keys/values, not just the final hidden state. Ablates +1.9 over hidden-state conditioning on LIBERO. (1 source)
- [Adaptive depth reasoning](concepts/learning/adaptive-depth-reasoning.md) — MolmoAct2-Think's latency fix for embodied CoT: predict a 10×10 VQ depth grid before acting, but **only recompute cells whose RGB patch changed** between timesteps; reasoning cost scales with scene change, not the full grid. (1 source)
- [Gradient-based planning (vs CEM)](concepts/world-models/gradient-based-planning.md) — backprop through a learned rollout instead of sampling candidates. Why it lost (**a gradient planner is an adversarial attack on its own world model**; and curved latents make the objective ill-conditioned), the two 2026 fixes, and the honest scoreboard: **CEM still wins on absolute success — GBP now reaches the old CEM bar at ~10% of the compute.** Also the page where the field's **three competing criteria for a good planning latent space** — identifiable, straight, sparse — are set side by side, none of them citing the others. (4 sources)
- [In-context robot learning](concepts/learning/in-context-robot-learning.md) — adapting a policy from information supplied at inference — a demonstration, or the robot's own recent experience — with **no weight update**. Covers **two mechanisms routinely conflated**: *demonstration-conditioned* ([S1](sources/skild-s1-blog.md): context holds a human demo of the **task**; in-context imitation) and *experience-conditioned* ([LocoFormer](sources/locoformer-paper.md): context holds the policy's own trials with this **body**; in-context RL, closer to online system identification). They answer different questions — "what should I do?" vs "what am I?" — and the evidence grades differ sharply: LocoFormer is peer-reviewed with a GRU ablation collapsing to 0.37, S1 is a vendor blog. Prehistory in [RMA](sources/rma-paper.md), which reached the same instinct (the useful latent is **behavioral, not physical**) four years earlier with a 0.5 s window and a hand-built bottleneck. (3 sources)
- [Test-time adaptation](concepts/learning/test-time-adaptation.md) — updating a model *during* deployment from a self-supervised signal the deployment stream provides for free. **Closed-loop control is its natural home**: the agent predicts, acts, and observes — that transition is exactly the pretraining loss, so a world model in a control loop generates labeled data about its own errors continuously. The rule worth keeping, from [AdaJEPA](entities/adajepa.md)'s shift taxonomy: **TTA repairs a miscalibrated model; it cannot repair observations that no longer carry the needed information** (recolor the anchor and no amount of transition data restores object identity). Introduces a sharper form of the [train-and-judge](concepts/learning/sim-to-real-transfer.md) circularity — a model fitted to the current episode cannot evaluate it. (2 sources)
- [Sim-to-real transfer](concepts/learning/sim-to-real-transfer.md) — bridging simulator-trained policies to real robots; "simulation bias" lineage back to [Kober 2013](sources/kober-rl-robotics-survey-2013.md); the 12.4% [BEHAVIOR-1K](entities/behavior-benchmark.md) hard end; plus the learned-simulator addition — **train and judge in the same model** and the score measures the model, not readiness (measured: evaluators run *high*). Now also the recursive case, and the **inverted approach** ([R2S2R](concepts/robotics/real-to-sim-to-real.md)): build the simulator from the task rather than closing a gap to a general one. (36 sources)
- [Scaling laws — VLAs and human data](concepts/learning/scaling-laws-vla.md) — the empirical data-vs-performance relationship for VLA pretraining; seeded from [EgoScale](sources/egoscale-paper.md)'s log-linear loss law (R² = 0.9983) on 20,854 hr of egocentric human video; LLM-side companion via Welch Labs Ch 6 (Kaplan 2020). (10 sources)
- [Chain of thought](concepts/learning/chain-of-thought.md) — intermediate-reasoning-token technique; origin (Wei 2022); zero-shot/self-consistency/ToT; modern reasoning models; embodied CoT in VLAs and S1/S2 splits; non-textual **depth-token** embodied CoT ([MolmoAct2](entities/molmoact2.md)). (5 sources)
- [Variational autoencoder (VAE)](concepts/learning/variational-autoencoder.md) — probabilistic latent space + ELBO loss + reparameterization trick ([Kingma & Welling 2013](sources/vae-paper.md)); superseded as a standalone generator, ubiquitous as infrastructure — the [DDPM](entities/ddpm.md) variational bound, [ACT](entities/act.md)'s CVAE, [VQ-BeT](entities/vq-bet.md)'s VQ-VAE, latent-diffusion latents, [World Models](sources/world-models-paper.md)' V model. (5 sources)
- [Energy-based models (EBMs)](concepts/learning/energy-based-models.md) — `E_θ(x, y)` low when compatible; inference is `argmin_y E`. Connects [IBC](entities/ibc.md), [JEPA](concepts/world-models/jepa.md) training, and [Kona](entities/kona.md) — three different applications of the same LeCun-line commitment to non-autoregressive learning. (5 sources)
- [Spectral theory of SSL](concepts/learning/spectral-theory-of-ssl.md) — SSL objectives *are* classical spectral embedding methods (VICReg ↔ Laplacian Eigenmaps, SimCLR ↔ MDS); the [Balestriero](entities/randall-balestriero.md)–[LeCun](entities/yann-lecun.md) math spine under SIGReg, [identifiability](concepts/world-models/identifiability.md), and the JEPA [generalization theory](sources/jepa-generalization-theory-paper.md). (3 sources)
- [Formal verification](concepts/learning/formal-verification.md) — machine-checkable proofs; "translate, propose, verify" pipeline; deterministic kernel as the hallucination cure. Used by [Aleph](entities/aleph.md). (3 sources)
- [Lean theorem prover](concepts/learning/lean-theorem-prover.md) — the verification substrate underneath [Aleph](entities/aleph.md); Mathlib; tactic + term mode; PutnamBench-native. (2 sources)
- [PutnamBench](concepts/learning/putnambench.md) — 672 Putnam Exam problems formalized in [Lean](concepts/learning/lean-theorem-prover.md); machine-checkable formal-reasoning benchmark; [Aleph](entities/aleph.md) at 99.4% (May 2026). (1 source)

### World models
- [World model](concepts/world-models/world-model.md) — umbrella concept: learned predictive model of environment dynamics (generative-video / JEPA / frozen-feature / model-based-RL / omnimodal WAM); MBRL lineage fully sourced (Ha&Schmidhuber → PlaNet → MuZero/EfficientZero → DayDreamer → DIAMOND). Now paired with the role-based [functional taxonomy](concepts/world-models/world-model-functional-taxonomy.md). (38 sources)
- [Joint-Embedding Predictive Architecture](concepts/world-models/jepa.md) — predict next-state representations, not pixels; anti-collapse taxonomy (frozen/EMA/var-cov/SIGReg/inverse-dynamics), spectral-graph theory, object-centric + hierarchical extensions. (36 sources)
- [FLARE (Future Latent Representation Alignment)](concepts/world-models/flare.md) — a JEPA-adjacent **auxiliary loss** adding implicit world modeling to a flow-matching VLA policy (align learnable future tokens to a future-observation embedding via an EMA teacher, λ=0.2); NVIDIA GEAR, adopted by [GR00T N1.5](sources/groot-n1_5.md). (3 sources)
- [Spatial intelligence](concepts/world-models/spatial-intelligence.md) — the **capability** ("understand a physical environment and use that to guide action"); world models are one pathway to it, not the thing itself. Craik 1943 → 1960s symbolic models → RL-by-internal-simulation + multimodal grounding. Carries the cheap progress probe: **move an object, leave the scene, return — is it still there?** Plus the substrate argument: *"language is an abstraction of the world, pixels are a projection of it, geometry/physics/dynamics are the world itself."* (4 sources)
- [SIGReg (Sketched Isotropic Gaussian Regularization)](concepts/world-models/sigreg.md) — the single-term anti-collapse regularizer behind the whole "Le-" line, mentioned on **58 pages** and previously filed nowhere. Why an **isotropic Gaussian** (minimax under task uncertainty), why *sketched* (direct multivariate normality testing is quadratic; a hyperspherical **Cramér–Wold** theorem makes 1-D directional tests equivalent), and why **Epps–Pulley** (CDF-based tests need sorting, which breaks SGD parallelism). Buys **1 hyperparameter** instead of 4–6, no stop-grad/EMA/schedulers, ~50 lines. Then the three 2026 results that bound it: **[SMWM](entities/smwm.md)** inverse dynamics wins 84-vs-59 on 3D, **[LpWM](entities/lpwm.md)**'s non-Gaussian sparse target models dynamics better, and **[stable-worldmodel](sources/stable-worldmodel-paper.md)** shows it buys no OOD robustness. (9 sources)
- [World-model functional taxonomy](concepts/world-models/world-model-functional-taxonomy.md) — **renderer** / **simulator** / **planner**, derived as the **three outputs of the POMDP loop** (observations / state / actions), making renderer and planner formal inverses. Now built on [its primary](sources/world-labs-functional-taxonomy.md) and **separating what the essay argues from what the HAI brief added** — the simulator-is-the-linchpin *dependency* argument is the essay's; interactivity-as-fourth-capability and *safeguards attach to deployment context, not model class* are the brief's. Maturity runs **opposite** to stakes, and the taxonomy is dissolving ([Cosmos 3](sources/cosmos-3-technical-report.md) is all three in one network). (5 sources)
- [World-model evaluation](concepts/world-models/world-model-evaluation.md) — two failure modes that look identical from outside (**visual plausibility trap** vs **sim-to-real gap**) plus the compound unique to learned models: **teaching to a flawed test**, where the same model trains and judges — **now measured** ([WorldArena](entities/worldarena.md)): learned evaluators *flatter* what they evaluate, ranking survives, levels don't. The 2026 benchmark landscape with primary sources, and the convergence with [robot policy evaluation](concepts/robotics/robot-policy-evaluation.md) — neither literature cites the other. Plus **the incommensurability problem**: the pixel and latent literatures use instruments that cannot run on each other's models — and the first shared probes (2026) find pixel fidelity and action recoverability **orthogonal**. (8 sources)
- [World-model simulators](concepts/world-models/world-model-simulators.md) — narrower companion to [World model](concepts/world-models/world-model.md): world-models-used-as-simulators. Three paradigms now — generative-video, JEPA, and **task-reconstructed generative simulators** ([R2S2R](concepts/robotics/real-to-sim-to-real.md)) that emit geometry and collision meshes rather than pixels. The thesis has independent measurement and **survives narrowed to one role** — RL environment yes, action planner no — with the pixels-vs-geometry line the only available reconciliation of WorldArena's *marginal data engine* against World Labs' *zero-real-data training*. (34 sources)
- [World-action model (WAM)](concepts/world-models/world-action-model.md) — one model doing forward dynamics + inverse dynamics + policy (predicting actions *and* their visual consequence); instances [Cosmos 3](sources/cosmos-3-technical-report.md), Dream*, GE-Sim2. (3 sources)
- [Identifiability](concepts/world-models/identifiability.md) — whether a learned latent space recovers the world's *actual* latent variables (linear identifiability = up to an orthogonal rotation). Proved for LeJEPA under stationary additive-noise Gaussian assumptions; the formal backing for latent-space planning. Paired with the warning that proved identifiability has not yet produced out-of-distribution robustness. Sibling to the JEPA [generalization theory](sources/jepa-generalization-theory-paper.md). (3 sources)
- [Instruction leakage](concepts/world-models/instruction-leakage.md) — an evaluation confound: a goal-conditioned world model whose instruction *names the scored quantity* can score high by **transcribing the instruction, not perceiving the scene**. Detect with goal-withheld + counterfactual probes; fix with goal-free dynamics. ([Wang et al. 2026](sources/grounding-spatial-relations-compact-wm-paper.md)). (1 source)
- [Learned latent space](concepts/world-models/latent-space.md) — vector space where a trained encoder represents inputs; substrate for JEPA prediction, DINOv2 features, VQ-BeT codebook. (26 sources)
- [Siamese network](concepts/world-models/siamese-network.md) — two weight-tied encoders + similarity/distance/predictor head; ancestor of every joint-embedding SSL system since 1993. (8 sources)

### Agents
- [Harness design for capacity-limited models](concepts/agents/local-model-harness-design.md) — a scaffold built for a frontier model is the wrong scaffold for a small one. Six moves: budget context at the **usable** window (~100K of an advertised 260K), page capability in as [skills](concepts/agents/agent-skills.md), compress the tool surface (**CLI tools over MCP servers**, whose definitions eat context), keep the **orchestrator deterministic** so the model only proposes, verify with trajectory-health hooks, and **fail closed** if the sandbox is unavailable. Plus escalation as an architecture: the advisor **returns text, never acts**. Same-model/same-box evidence that the harness is worth **65.1% vs 13.9%** and 41× the tokens. One vendor-run source, document-domain — but it is the layer below the wiki's [on-robot agent](syntheses/agents/on-device-and-on-robot-agents.md) coverage. (1 source)
- [Frontier-agent containment incidents, summer 2026](syntheses/agents/frontier-agent-containment-incidents-2026.md) — **reads the four primaries [NVIDIA](sources/nvidia-where-security-fits-agent-stack.md) cites in one unsourced sentence, and finds the compression loses the distinction its own thesis needs.** Only OpenAI's was a containment failure; Anthropic's models walked through a **misconfiguration** and AISI's were granted internet access deliberately — which is a *stronger* argument for infrastructure-side enforcement, since **a prompt asserting "you have no internet access" is the purest possible behavioral control and it failed by being false**. Six findings: scope not intent is the enforceable thing (no goal-seeking in any incident, so a control keyed to adversarial intent catches none of them); **what stopped the worst outcomes was people**; situational awareness is the measured variable and it oscillates *within* a run; **context compaction launders hedged beliefs into settled facts** — a safety surface no embodied-agent page here covers; the **supply chain is the shared blast radius** and this wiki is in it (PyPI dependency confusion on 15 systems; HF entered through dataset configs); and **the defender's guardrails bound the defender** while the attacker ran with classifiers off. Closes with what does *not* transfer to robots: every one of these was mediated by discrete, loggable, gateable actions — a joint trajectory has no transcript. (2026-08-23)
- [Symbolic task planning (PDDL)](concepts/agents/symbolic-task-planning.md) — the 50-year-old formal answer to "human-readable, embodiment-agnostic action specification." Complete without names for a solver — yet renaming predicates collapses GPT-4 from 0.90 to 0.10. Says nothing about motion, which is the whole problem. (1 source)
- [LLM-agent architecture](concepts/agents/llm-agent-architecture.md) — LLM-emits-tool-calls control pattern; MCP and A2A as inter-agent/tool-access protocols; **the missing guardrail layer** between planner and skill library → [Guardrails for robot agents](syntheses/agents/guardrails-for-robot-agents.md); the override decision now **measured** (supervising a VLA hurts in-distribution, helps on novel tasks); the [strange-loop](concepts/agents/strange-loops-and-self-reference.md) reading of the same pattern. **Where the tool schema comes from is the axis that moved**: hand-written manifest (stretch_ai / ROSOrin / OpenClaw) → agent-authored library ([Waddle](entities/waddle-labs.md), [CaP-X](entities/cap-x.md)) → **introspected from running code** ([DimOS](entities/dimos.md)). (43 sources)
- [Agent skills (portable SKILL.md)](concepts/agents/agent-skills.md) — vendor-shipped `SKILL.md` + helper-script bundles that give an agent domain-correct procedures (vs. generic advice) and make deployment agent-drivable; cross-agent portable (Claude Code / Cursor / Codex). Robotics instances: [Jetson Device Skills](entities/jetson-device-skills.md), [Halos](entities/nvidia-halos.md) deploy skills, FRC team skill files. Now contrasted against **agent-authored** libraries mined from experience ([CaP-X](entities/cap-x.md)) and from diagnosed failures ([ASPIRE](entities/aspire.md)), and against a **third sense of the word** — [DimOS](entities/dimos.md)'s `@skill`-decorated robot methods discovered by RPC and republished over MCP, where the artifact is live code rather than a file. (6 sources)
- [Code as policy](concepts/agents/code-as-policy.md) — the [LLM-agent](concepts/agents/llm-agent-architecture.md) sub-pattern where the action vocabulary is **executable code**, not a fixed tool menu: the model *writes the program* that runs the robot. **The full 2022–2026 lineage is ingested** (nine papers, 2026-08-03). The core empirical claim never changed: code agents **degrade gracefully where trained policies fall off a cliff**, and lose to them in-distribution — demonstrated against CLIPort (2022), primitive-parameterizing CaP (2023), and internet-scale VLAs (2026). What improved is *feedback*: open-loop plans → textual feedback → self-verification → visual differencing → per-primitive traces, each step buying more than the model upgrades beside it. Two things never moved: **the primitive set is still ported per embodiment, and nobody reports cost.** (10 sources)

- [Strange loops and self-reference](concepts/agents/strange-loops-and-self-reference.md) — [Hofstadter](entities/douglas-hofstadter.md)'s self-closing hierarchy applied to agents: the model's output re-enters as its input, and *"complexity is a property of the loop itself."* Holds the **deflationary counter** — a loop in the transcript is not necessarily a loop in the system — and scores Masood's three prerequisites (persistent self-model / grounded world-model / self-monitoring) against what the wiki actually measures. All three come out weak. Also names the unposed question: robot learning already trains on its own generative output ([DreamGen](entities/dreamgen.md), [MimicGen](entities/mimicgen.md), neural trajectories). (2 sources)

### Economics
- [Three thinking styles](concepts/economics/three-thinking-styles.md) — [Jordan](entities/michael-i-jordan.md)'s computational ⊕ inferential ⊕ economic framework; the missing piece is the *tripartite* blend; "no Maxwell's equations… we are winging it." (1 source)
- [Collectivist AI / AI-as-market](concepts/economics/collectivist-ai.md) — AI best modeled as a **market** of strategic human + non-human participants; producer vs consumer roles; three-way and three-layer market archetypes. (1 source)
- [Mechanism design & statistical contract theory](concepts/economics/mechanism-design.md) — mechanism design = inverse game theory; Stackelberg equilibria; the e-value ⟺ incentive-compatible-contract result (Bates et al. 2024). (1 source)
- [Prediction-powered inference (PPI)](concepts/economics/prediction-powered-inference.md) — correct a foundation model's biased uncertainty using local ground truth (Angelopoulos et al. 2023); reinterpreted as an implicit incentive mechanism. (1 source)

### Artificial Life
- [Artificial life and the emergence of self-replication](concepts/alife/artificial-life-and-self-replication.md) — self-replication + open-ended complexity arise from random interacting self-modifying programs with **no fitness function** (Agüera y Arcas et al.); "high-order entropy" detects the pre-life→life transition; SUBLEQ counterexample. Now also covers the **biological** counterpart ([Xenobots](entities/xenobots.md) kinematic self-replication) + the programming-game / digital-replicator lineage now ingested **end-to-end** ([Darwin](sources/darwin-1961-bell-labs-game.md) 1961 → [Core War](entities/core-war.md) 1984 → [Tierra](entities/tierra.md)/[Avida](entities/avida.md) 1991–94 → [Computational Life](sources/computational-life-self-replicating-programs-paper.md) 2024); plus the Avida evolution-of-complexity capstone ([Lenski et al. 2003](sources/lenski-2003-evolutionary-origin-complex-features.md)). (19 sources)
- [Evolutionary computation](concepts/alife/evolutionary-computation.md) — gradient-free, population-based optimization; co-designs **morphology + behavior** (can't backprop through "what shape?"); the design engine behind [Xenobots](entities/xenobots.md). Contrast with SGD/RL. (3 sources)
- [Cellular automata](concepts/alife/cellular-automata.md) — grid + shared local rule → emergent global structure, no central controller. [Conway's Game of Life](entities/game-of-life.md) (Class 4, universal) + [Wolfram](entities/stephen-wolfram.md)'s elementary CAs / Rule 30; **computational irreducibility**; the **construction-vs-search** axis for making patterns. The foundational substrate of this branch. (1 source)
- [Neural Cellular Automata (NCA)](concepts/alife/neural-cellular-automata.md) — cellular automata with a **learned** shared local update rule; self-organize into images/shapes/textures with regeneration + robustness; the learnable wing of self-organization. Mordvintsev lineage. (3 sources)
- [Flocking and boids](concepts/alife/flocking-and-boids.md) — Reynolds' 1986 boids; three local steering rules (separation/alignment/cohesion) → emergent flocking; "edge of chaos"; bridges to swarm intelligence/robotics. Sibling emergence model to Computational Life. (6 sources)

### Safety
- [Semantic safety](concepts/safety/semantic-safety.md) — the robot-safety layer that is about **meaning, not force**: a knife not pointed at a human, a soft toy not on a hot stove. Empty before language models because robots had no semantics to get wrong. Measured by [ASIMOV](entities/asimov-benchmark.md), probed by [RoboART](entities/roboart.md) and [Veo](entities/veo.md), and contrasted against [provable safe exploration](sources/safely-learning-dynamical-systems-paper.md) — which covers only linear/polynomial systems, so **the formal and empirical wings do not meet**. Currently measured, sometimes predicted, **not yet enforced**. (8 sources)

- [AI safety and alignment](concepts/safety/ai-safety-alignment.md) — corrigibility, broadly safe behaviors, hard constraints, catastrophic risk framing; the training-time pole vs. the deployment-time pole ([guardrails](concepts/safety/ai-guardrails.md)); connects to agentic robot deployments. Now also covers **RSP capability thresholds** — where the wiki's safety and robotics threads meet, since *embodiment is tracked as a scaling-policy variable*; plus **access level as a system property** — an eval result without its [abstraction level](concepts/robotics/control-abstraction-levels.md) is not a result. (14 sources)
- [World-model governance](concepts/safety/world-model-governance.md) — the **third governance object**: content → authority to act → **validity of the learned environment**. Three pillars (shared action-data infrastructure / proportional safeguards / public-sector capacity); the **procurement trap** (the best simulator for a system is the vendor's own, so the buyer depends on the vendor to both build *and* validate); export controls possibly aimed at the wrong chokepoint; spatial privacy as an **inference** problem. Independently reaches [control abstraction levels](concepts/robotics/control-abstraction-levels.md)' conclusion that access level is the safety variable. (1 source)
- [Corrigibility](concepts/safety/corrigibility.md) — the corrigibility dial (fully corrigible ↔ fully autonomous); asymmetric cost argument; galaxy-brained reasoning risk; agentic deployment implications. (1 source)
- [Mechanistic interpretability](concepts/safety/mechanistic-interpretability.md) — reading and steering features inside trained neural nets; sparse autoencoders (Anthropic / Templeton et al. 2024); Chris Olah's "dark matter of interpretability" (~1% of concepts extracted). Seeded by [Welch Labs Illustrated Guide Ch 7](sources/welchlabs-illustrated-guide-to-ai.md). (1 source)
- [AI guardrails](concepts/safety/ai-guardrails.md) — the **deployment-time** safety pole (vs. training-time alignment): build→deploy→run lifecycle; five rail types; the content-safety / topic-control / jailbreak-detect / PII library; **and the gap** — the guard *models* all classify text, robots emit *tool calls*, the execution rail that would catch them ships **empty**, and the physical-safety stack ([ISO 13482](concepts/robotics/robot-safety-standards.md)) is disjoint from all of it. Anthropic's [robotics eval](sources/anthropic-how-claude-performs-on-robotics-tasks.md) now argues for exactly that empty rail from the capability side (**scoped physical access**). (8 sources)
- [AI red-teaming and LLM vulnerability scanning](concepts/safety/ai-red-teaming.md) — jailbreaks, prompt injection, harmful-content elicitation; [garak](entities/garak.md) as "nmap for LLMs"; **embodiment makes prompt injection physical** (leave a note where the robot will look) — and no ingested source red-teams an embodied agent. Now also disambiguates the **two genres published under "red team"**: adversarial probing vs [uplift evaluation](concepts/safety/ai-uplift.md). (6 sources)
- [AI uplift studies](concepts/safety/ai-uplift.md) — randomized AI-arm vs no-AI-arm measurement of *human* performance; scores the human-plus-model system, not the model. The **uplift-precedes-autonomy** argument makes it a leading indicator for scaling-policy thresholds. Grounded in [Project Fetch](sources/anthropic-project-fetch-robot-dog.md) **and its [Phase Two](sources/anthropic-project-fetch-phase-two.md) re-run**, which tested the premise on the same ladder ten months later (2× human uplift → model alone at 18.9× the assisted humans). Flags that the wiki has ~30 capability benchmarks and **one** human-in-the-loop measurement. (3 sources)

### Robotics
- [Onboard robot service architecture](concepts/robotics/onboard-robot-service-architecture.md) — the layer *below* middleware: how a shipped robot's software splits into processes, which of them must survive a dead control loop, who may write to a motor, and how a release reaches a machine you cannot physically touch. Eight patterns from the only public consumer-robot runtime in the wiki ([Microduck](entities/microduck.md)) — split by failure domain not function, make the dangerous state unrepresentable, **keep the safety layer judgmentally minimal so recovery never has to bypass it**, features-not-frames, atomic health-gated release swap, build the updater first, observability as a contract, one API over many transports. Single-source and provisional. (1 source)
- [Runtime failure detection for generative policies](concepts/robotics/runtime-failure-detection.md) — deciding **during** a rollout whether the policy is going to fail, which is neither safety filtering (prevents harm, blind to task progress) nor evaluation (after the fact, across rollouts). Both ingested methods ([Sentinel](sources/sentinel-paper.md), [FAIL-Detect](sources/fail-detect-paper.md)) train on **successes only**, because failure modes aren't enumerable — one policy on one pick-and-place task produced **six** qualitatively different failures. Now spans the full arc — two monitors that detect ([Sentinel](sources/sentinel-paper.md), [FAIL-Detect](sources/fail-detect-paper.md)), one predictor ([FIPER](sources/fiper-paper.md)), one intervention ([FOREWARN](sources/forewarn-paper.md)). Carries the mechanisms worth stealing (**STAC**'s action-chunk overlap, **`logpZO`**'s check-for-noise, **ACE**'s entropy-not-variance, FOREWARN's **narrate-the-latents**), the **contradiction** over whether a sampling-based score is deployable (36–44×), and the shared limits: the conformal guarantee bounds **false alarms, not misses**, and none of them runs on a generalist policy. (4 sources)
- [Safety filters for learned policies](concepts/robotics/safety-filters.md) — the hub for the wiki's three ingested runtime-safety mechanisms ([deployed diff-IK envelope](concepts/robotics/operational-space-control.md), [OSCBF](sources/oscbf-paper.md), [PACS](sources/pacs-paper.md)), with the finding none of them states alone: **the axis that decides whether a filter destroys a learned policy is path-consistent vs path-deviating, not how strong its guarantee is** (0.72 vs 0.04 task success, same constraints, same robot). Carries the taxonomy (optimization-based vs reachability-based; post-hoc vs injected into denoising), the two "minimal in what?" refinements (**task consistency**, **path consistency**), the **safe success** metric, and the three things none of them do: perception, semantic safety, certification. (4 sources)
- [Operational space control](concepts/robotics/operational-space-control.md) — Khatib 1987 task-space control, and (the reason it is here) **the constrained-QP layer that makes learned-policy rollouts safe in the MIT/TRI stack**. The policy never commands the robot: it emits end-effector poses into a **differential-IK QP at ~1 kHz** whose *hard constraints* are arm–arm collision, the table, an end-effector keep-out region, and joint limits — *"particularly valuable for **safeguarding the learned policy** during hardware deployment"* ([Diffusion Policy](sources/diffusion-policy-paper.md) App. D.1; same architecture in [TRI LBM](sources/tri-lbm-paper.md)). Objectives are costs, limits are constraints, and the guard re-solves **~100× per 10 Hz policy decision** — so the [rate gap](syntheses/platforms/control-rate-ladder.md) usually read as *the policy is too slow* is also *what makes a stochastic policy deployable*. Explicitly **not** a certified safety filter: model-based and geometric, no perception, no CBF, no guarantee — so the page also carries [OSCBF](sources/oscbf-paper.md), the Stanford CBF version that *does* prove forward invariance (168 constraints at ~3 kHz) and introduces **task consistency**: filter the task output, not the control input. The two halves have never been combined — one stack has the policies, the other has the proof. (3 sources)
- [Visual relocalization and mapping](concepts/robotics/visual-relocalization-and-mapping.md) — **the tradition this wiki had no page for despite depending on it.** World models ask *what happens if I act*; SLAM asks *where am I and what shape is this place*. Covers the mapping / localization / **drift** split, optimization-based ([RTAB-Map](entities/rtab-map.md) + [GTSAM](entities/gtsam.md)) vs **feed-forward** ([LingBot-Map](entities/lingbot-map.md), [Niantic](entities/niantic-spatial.md)'s ACE→2026 arc), and the representation ladder up to **Gaussian splats**. Names what is still uncovered: NeRF, splatting-as-method, ORB-SLAM, factor graphs, Dellaert. (3 sources)
- [Behavior trees](concepts/robotics/behavior-trees.md) — six node types, ticked from the root; the dominant *deployed* action-composition formalism. Makes the **composition** readable while staying agnostic about the action — **the only readable-*and*-portable option in the [action-language spectrum](syntheses/agents/action-representation-languages.md)**. Formally generalizes FSMs / subsumption / decision trees; safety proofs and stochastic reliability analysis included. A ready-made candidate for the [execution rail that ships empty](syntheses/agents/guardrails-for-robot-agents.md). (1 source)
- [6-DOF grasp generation](concepts/robotics/six-dof-grasp-generation.md) — **the module this wiki depended on and never had a page for.** Predict SE(3) poses at which closing a gripper lifts an object: sample (now diffusion over SE(3)) then rank with a discriminator, trained on 10⁸–10⁹ **simulation-labelled** grasps (ACRONYM + [Isaac Sim](entities/nvidia-isaac-sim.md)) — one of the few manipulation problems where the bottleneck is **GPU-hours, not teleoperation**, which is why a grasp model sees 10⁴× more data than a [VLA](concepts/learning/vla-models.md). Carries the cross-embodiment ladder (direct transfer 0.126 → retargeting 0.398 → gripper conditioning 0.506), the finding that **12 hand-designed numbers beat every learned gripper-mesh encoder** because grasping conditions on *what the gripper does* not *what it looks like*, the "procedural beats real" training-distribution result (the same shape as [RoboTwin 2.0](sources/robotwin2-paper.md)'s diversity-not-fidelity finding), and the planner-side reframe that makes it practical: in clutter most grasps die to collision/IK rejection, so **spatial coverage of the candidate set** binds harder than per-grasp precision — which predicts the approach strains most on **low-DoF arms**. (3 sources)
- [Assistive robotics](concepts/robotics/assistive-robotics.md) — robots helping disabled/elderly users regain autonomy; systematic review; three research themes; autonomy finding. (26 sources)
- [Collaborative robots (cobots)](concepts/robotics/collaborative-robots.md) — industrial arms that share a workspace with humans without fencing (force-limited, lead-through-programmed); the classical-automation lineage of "AI robotics." Vendors: [Techman](entities/techman-robot.md), Universal Robots, [Standard Bots](entities/standard-bots.md); humanoids ([Agile ONE](entities/agile-one.md)) inherit the "safe near humans" contract. (2 sources)
- [Robot combat sports as a development testbed](concepts/robotics/robot-combat-sports.md) — humanoid fighting leagues (2025–2026, China) as adversarial [whole-body-control](concepts/robotics/whole-body-control.md) stress-tests + spectacle; primary instance [URKL](sources/urkl-robot-combat-league.md) / [EngineAI T800](entities/engineai-t800.md); autonomy-vs-teleop is the open caveat. (2 sources)
- [Aging in place](concepts/robotics/aging-in-place.md) — "staying in your own home as you get older"; the demand-side ADL/IADL needs taxonomy (NIA) that motivates in-home assistive robotics; mainstream elder-care guidance names no robots. (1 source)
- [Robot safety standards (ISO 13482)](concepts/robotics/robot-safety-standards.md) — machinery-safety framework for robots near untrained people; 2025 "service robots" retitle; certification precedents; the deterministic-safety vs learned-policy collision. (12 sources)
- [Robot security (robot cybersecurity)](concepts/robotics/robot-security.md) — securing the robot *system* (OS/middleware/network/firmware) vs. safety (physical harm); [RSF](sources/aliasrobotics-rsf-github.md)'s 4-layer assessment; where classical infosec meets the LLM-agent [input-rail / guardrails](concepts/safety/ai-guardrails.md) thread on [ROS 2](entities/ros2.md). (2 sources)
- [End-user robot programming](concepts/robotics/end-user-robot-programming.md) — enabling non-experts to customize robot behavior; EUP approaches; sense of agency evidence; Stretch SE2 transfer. (7 sources)
- [Accessible robot communication](concepts/robotics/accessible-robot-communication.md) — robot output-interface design for non-visual users; mixed-initiative narration findings; 6 design guidelines (Huh et al. 2026). (4 sources)
- [Optimal control](concepts/robotics/optimal-control.md) — minimize a cost over trajectory-control pairs subject to dynamics. Brachystochrone (1697) → Euler–Lagrange → Hamilton–Jacobi → Pontryagin's Maximum Principle (1956) → Bellman DP → modern LQR / MPC / iLQR / CEM / learned-WM-OC. The "RL = approximate OC under uncertainty" bridge — plus [GCS](concepts/robotics/graphs-of-convex-sets.md) as the row that shows what the dynamics constraint *costs*: drop it and you get global optimality with a proof. (21 sources)
- [Motion planning (classical)](concepts/robotics/motion-planning.md) — computing collision-free paths given an explicit world model: search-based (A*), sampling-based (PRM/RRT*, OMPL), optimization-based (CHOMP/TrajOpt/[GCS](concepts/robotics/graphs-of-convex-sets.md)), + ML-for-planning. The deployed core of classical robotics; what learned policies replace. Now carries the correction that "local minima" is the wrong criticism of GCS — **"restricted problem class"** is. (2 sources)
- [Graphs of convex sets (GCS)](concepts/robotics/graphs-of-convex-sets.md) — vertex = convex set, edge = convex length function; shortest path picks a discrete route *and* the continuous values along it, jointly. Its MICP relaxes so tightly that **one LP/SOCP plus randomized rounding usually returns the global optimum**, and `C_relax ≤ C_opt ≤ C_round` yields a **free per-instance suboptimality certificate** — the structural opposite of [policy evaluation by rollouts](concepts/robotics/robot-policy-evaluation.md). Generalizes PRM (a sample inflated into a region). **In production at [Dexai Robotics](entities/dexai-robotics.md)**, replacing a tuned PRM — narrowly, in the fixed-workcell/precompute-once regime where its restrictions cost nothing. Hand-seeding has since been replaced by **visibility-graph clique cover**; contact and task-space constraints have research answers (spectrahedra as vertices; analytical-IK regions on manifolds); the dual is a **value-function lower bound**, turning plans into policies. Scaling limit, per its author: the graph explodes for dexterous hands. **The framework is not robotics-specific**: its own target application is **hybrid/PWA optimal control** (17 min → 7.1 s vs the prior formulation), and the technique extends to other graph problems with continuous vertex positions. (4 sources)
- [Task and motion planning (TAMP)](concepts/robotics/task-and-motion-planning.md) — long-horizon tasks via symbolic task planning + low-level motion planning; the decomposition that keeps re-emerging inside LLM-planner stacks and System 2 / System 1 VLAs. (1 source)
- [Robot policy evaluation](concepts/robotics/robot-policy-evaluation.md) — how you establish one policy beats another, and how little of the published record supports its own rankings. Clopper-Pearson **±2 pp ⇒ ≈1,030 rollouts vs ~70 typical**; saturation above 90%; **memorization** ([LIBERO-PRO](sources/libero-pro-paper.md): >90% → 0.0%) as a second, differently-fixed failure mode; **[RoboArena](entities/roboarena.md)'s pairwise-preference alternative** (r≈0.95 vs 0.60); graded scores / **SPARC** / failure-event logs / **NPE**; competency tagging; *vague instructions consistently fail*; **no policy beyond ~4 complex subtasks**. **Directly qualifies the wiki's own VLA success-rate tables.** Now six paradigms, with [R2S2R](concepts/robotics/real-to-sim-to-real.md) as a **checkpoint-screening layer** — and the structural point none of them state alone: making simulated evaluation cheap does not make the real ground truth it is validated against any cheaper, so surrogate evaluators stay defensible as rankers and indefensible as measurers. (13 sources)
- [Real-to-sim-to-real (R2S2R)](concepts/robotics/real-to-sim-to-real.md) — invert the starting point of [sim-to-real](concepts/learning/sim-to-real-transfer.md): build the simulator **from the specific real task**, validate by **matched open-loop rollouts**, train and screen in it, return to hardware. What it buys that hardware cannot sell: no environment resets, **counterfactual supervision**, and the **teleop-speed ceiling** — demonstrations are collected slower than a human does the task, and you can't replay faster because *gravity doesn't rescale*. What it does not remove: the real-data cost, which moves into world construction and is never quantified. Its own authors say it is a **flywheel with a physics prior, not a replacement for real data** — the blog headline oversells them. Runs straight into the **train-and-judge** failure mode it is otherwise well placed to fix. (5 sources)
- [Control abstraction levels](concepts/robotics/control-abstraction-levels.md) — direct / programmatic / policy / RL-supervision. Frontier-model capability **inverts** with depth: good at writing robot software, bad at *being* robot software (83 Hz needed vs 0.2–0.4 Hz). Deference calibration is the supervision skill; **access level is a safety variable**, not a deployment detail. (4 sources)
- [Whole-body control (WBC)](concepts/robotics/whole-body-control.md) — coordinating all of a high-DoF humanoid's joints into stable motion, usually via RL motion-tracking of retargeted mocap; the low-level System 1 beneath a VLA. Model-level scaling ([SONIC](sources/sonic-paper.md), [MotionBricks](sources/motionbricks-paper.md)) vs. data-level cluster+distill ([BumbleBee](sources/bumblebee-experts-to-generalist-wbc.md)); delta-action sim-to-real; [Unitree G1](entities/unitree-g1.md) as shared benchmark. (9 sources)
- [AprilTags](concepts/robotics/apriltags.md) — visual fiducial markers for 6-DOF pose estimation; standard in FRC and research robotics. (4 sources)
- [Single-stage object detection (the YOLO paradigm)](concepts/robotics/single-stage-object-detection.md) — detection in **one forward pass** as regression, vs two-stage propose-then-classify. Founded by [YOLOv1](sources/yolo-you-only-look-once-2016.md); four recurring axes (anchors added-then-removed, multi-scale necks, free training tricks, NMS→[NMS-free](sources/yolov10-nms-free-2024.md)). Its durable weakness — small objects in groups — is exactly where [heatmaps](concepts/robotics/heatmap-object-localization.md) win. (5 sources)
- [SAHI (Slicing Aided Hyper Inference)](concepts/robotics/sahi-slicing-inference.md) — inference-time small-object-detection technique: slice image into overlapping patches, detect per patch, merge via NMS; model-agnostic, wraps [YOLO](entities/ultralytics-yolo.md)-class detectors for edge CCTV / aerial. (2 sources)
- [Heatmap-based object localization](concepts/robotics/heatmap-object-localization.md) — dense per-pixel confidence map instead of bounding boxes; peak = object position. The representation that makes **2–12 px** objects tractable ([TrackNet](entities/tracknet.md) 97.5 F1 vs YOLOv7 68.0). Rival/complement to [SAHI](concepts/robotics/sahi-slicing-inference.md): change the *output*, not the *inference procedure*. Also the standard representation in human pose estimation. (9 sources)
- [Detection evaluation metrics](concepts/robotics/detection-evaluation-metrics.md) — why scoring a **detector** ≠ scoring a classifier: the TP/FP/FN counts come from a **matching** step (IoU or distance threshold + greedy assignment + detection cap), and precision/recall/F1/AP all inherit its hidden parameters. Ties [Padilla](sources/padilla-object-detection-metrics.md), [COCO](sources/coco-detection-eval.md), [TrackNet](entities/tracknet.md), and [pinball_tracker](sources/pinball-tracker-repo.md); explains F1-at-one-operating-point vs AP-over-all. (9 sources)
- [Motion attention (frame differencing as a learnable prompt)](concepts/robotics/motion-attention.md) — make motion **explicit**: absolute frame differencing + a learnable power-normalization layer → attention maps multiplied into visual features. Buys recall, pays precision, ~free at inference. Rehabilitates a classical signal by making its interpretation learned — but **assumes a static camera**, the blocker for robot-mounted reuse. (3 sources)
- [Agentic UAVs](concepts/robotics/agentic-uavs.md) — autonomous aerial systems with goal-driven behavior; 4-layer architecture; 8 domains; adaptive control; MBRL on real racing quadrotors ([S5WM](sources/s5wm-paper.md)). (8 sources)
- [Swarm intelligence](concepts/robotics/swarm-intelligence.md) — decentralized collectives with emergent global behavior from local rules; bridges [boids](concepts/alife/flocking-and-boids.md)/ALife to UAV swarms; C2 taxonomy, swarm metaheuristics (PSO/ACO), 1000-drone guidance, anti-drone. (3 sources)

### Bio
- [Biomechanical simulation](concepts/bio/biomechanical-simulation.md) — physics-based simulation of an animal body; lineage *C. elegans* → Hydra → virtual rodent → NeuroMechFly v1/v2 → flybody. (7 sources)
- [Connectome](concepts/bio/connectome.md) — complete wiring diagram of a nervous system; *C. elegans* → fly hemibrain → FlyWire → [H01 human cortex](entities/h01-connectome.md) → mouse. (4 sources)

## Syntheses

### Curriculum
- [Robot-learning curriculum — from neurons to LeWorldModel](syntheses/curriculum/robot-learning-curriculum.md) — 14-module curriculum hub; **all 14 modules drafted**. (2026-05-10)
- [Curriculum Module 1 — Neural networks and training](syntheses/curriculum/curriculum-01-neural-networks.md) — Tier 1; neuron, MLP, backprop, BN/LN, residuals, Adam. Anchor: tiny MLP digit classifier. (2026-05-10)
- [Curriculum Module 2 — CNNs and visual representation learning](syntheses/curriculum/curriculum-02-cnns.md) — Tier 1; convolution, pooling, ResNet, ImageNet pretrain + fine-tune, visual encoders. Anchor: ResNet-18 features on PushT. (2026-05-10)
- [Curriculum Module 3 — Sequence models, attention, and transformers](syntheses/curriculum/curriculum-03-attention-and-transformers.md) — Tier 1; attention, MHA, transformer blocks, ViT, positional encoding, causal masking. Anchor: tiny transformer on PushT patches. (2026-05-10)
- [Curriculum Module 4 — Self-supervised learning and embeddings](syntheses/curriculum/curriculum-04-self-supervised-learning.md) — Tier 1; SSL taxonomy, representation collapse, anti-collapse families (EMA, VICReg, frozen, multi-fix, SIGReg). Anchor: VICReg on CIFAR with/without regularizer. (2026-05-10)
- [Curriculum Module 5 — Generative modeling fundamentals (DDPM, full math)](syntheses/curriculum/curriculum-05-generative-models.md) — Tier 2. Full ELBO → `L_simple` derivation; KL bounds; ε-parameterization; noise schedules; DDIM sampling; classifier-free guidance derivation. Anchor: train tiny DDPM on MNIST + paper-derive `L_simple` from ELBO. (2026-05-10)
- [Curriculum Module 6 — Imitation learning and behavior cloning](syntheses/curriculum/curriculum-06-imitation-learning.md) — IL/BC frame, multi-modal failure mode, distribution shift / DAgger, action chunking + receding-horizon control, PushT setup. (2026-05-10)
- [Curriculum Module 7 — BC lineage on PushT (IBC → BeT → DP)](syntheses/curriculum/curriculum-07-bc-lineage-pusht.md) — multi-modal-action problem + IBC/BeT/Diffusion Policy + bridge to world models; anchor exercise on PushT. (2026-05-10)
- [Curriculum Module 8 — Reinforcement learning vocabulary](syntheses/curriculum/curriculum-08-rl-vocabulary.md) — light/vocabulary-only RL coverage (MDP, return, value, policy, REINFORCE → PPO, DQN, MFRL vs MBRL, Dreamer-class latent imagination); supporting module for the LeWM Dreamer / TD-MPC baselines. (2026-05-10)
- [Curriculum Module 9 — Vision-Language-Action models](syntheses/curriculum/curriculum-09-vla.md) — closes the policy-side reading chain (6 → 7 → 9). VLA structural definition; VLAs are not world models; action-head taxonomy; major 2026 VLAs; S1/S2 hierarchical pattern; VLA-JEPA as Module-11 cross-over. (2026-05-10)
- [Curriculum Module 10 — World models, broad](syntheses/curriculum/curriculum-10-world-models.md) — functional definition; four families (generative-video / JEPA / frozen-feature / MBRL); MPC + CEM + gradient-based planning; horizon vs compounding error; LeWM positioned in the taxonomy. (2026-05-10)
- [Curriculum Module 11 — JEPA in depth](syntheses/curriculum/curriculum-11-jepa-deep.md) — joint-embedding architecture; collapse-prevention zoo (EMA, VICReg, frozen encoder, multi-fix, SIGReg); V-JEPA progression; DINO-WM frozen-feature variants; JEPA-WMs first-real-Franka; LeWM-vs-V-JEPA-2 axis-by-axis. (2026-05-10)
- [Curriculum Module 12 — LeWorldModel deep-dive (with full SIGReg math)](syntheses/curriculum/curriculum-12-lewm-deep-dive.md) — the destination module. LeWM section by section; full SIGReg derivation (random projections + Epps–Pulley + Cramér–Wold + backprop); architecture (incl. the BN-after-CLS trick); CEM-MPC; four-env results; latent probing + VoE; what it means for the JEPA program. (2026-05-10)
- [Curriculum Module 13 — Home robotics deployment reality](syntheses/curriculum/curriculum-13-home-robotics-deployment.md) — the 89.4 / 12.4 gap; Stretch convergence; RUM + OK-Robot as strongest current results; PAR / EUP / autonomy-preference framing; where LeWM-class techniques plausibly fit (and don't). Anchor: pick LeWM-on-Stretch vs DINO-WM-on-Stretch. (2026-05-10)
- [Curriculum Module 14 — Capstone (paper-first, hardware-second)](syntheses/curriculum/curriculum-14-capstone.md) — the capstone.
