Phase A: reproduce LeWM PushT + 5–10 page experiment-design memo for a Stretch experiment. Phase B (gated): execute on real Stretch, compare to a Diffusion Policy baseline. The curriculum is completable on phase A alone. (2026-05-10)

### Platforms
- [Prevention, detection, intervention](syntheses/platforms/prevention-detection-intervention.md) — **the runtime stack a deployed policy needs**, from six sources that are building three layers of it without citing each other's role. States the rule that spans them — *an intervention that stays inside the policy's own output distribution costs almost nothing; one that leaves it costs almost everything* (0.04 vs 0.72) — and the four things wrong with every layer at once: they all **assume perception they don't do**, every guarantee bounds **false alarms rather than misses**, detection plateaus at **~0.78** while prevention is near-solved, and no sample size here can rank anything. Ends on the hazard nobody has hit because nobody has run two layers together: **a safety filter braking for a human is indistinguishable, to a progress monitor, from a policy that has stalled.** (2026-08-16)
- [Sourccey vs XLeRobot](syntheses/platforms/sourccey-vs-xlerobot.md) — two answers to the same $1k household manipulator, a year apart. Sourccey wins on **shipped policy, vertical lift, standard LiDAR, and integration**; XLeRobot wins on **2.4× battery energy, full reproducibility (BOM/URDF/docs), permissive licensing, compute headroom, and having a price**. Names the two tensions Vulcan doesn't: open-loop wheels under a SLAM roadmap, and a 0.9 B policy advertised beside a Pi 5. The research question hiding inside a consumer product: **can a soft-prompted 6-DOF-pretrained VLA transfer to a 5-DOF arm?** (2026-08-13)
- [Success-rate audit — which of this wiki's policy comparisons survive their sample sizes](syntheses/platforms/vla-success-rate-audit.md) — applies the [RoboLab](sources/nvidia-robolab-evaluation-blog.md) rollout bar to the wiki's own tables. **The entire top of the LIBERO table (96.5–98.1) is one statistical tie** — separating two policies at ~97% needs >1.8 pp and the cluster spans 1.6 pp; "top of the wiki's table" was never supportable. Resolves the MolmoAct2-Think open question (**the +0.9 is not established**, p=0.35). Also corrects the bar itself: **1,030 rollouts is the n at a *90%* success rate; at 50% it is ~2,450**, so the standard is *more* demanding exactly where real-world manipulation lives. Every conclusion the wiki actually reasons from rests on a large gap and survives. (2026-07-27)
- [The control-rate ladder — LLMs, VLAs, and servo loops on one axis](syntheses/platforms/control-rate-ladder.md) — **every frequency number in the wiki on one axis**, requirement-side and measured-side: MotionBricks 15k FPS / Franka 1 kHz servo / WBC 500 Hz / Helix S1 200 Hz / **Anthropic's 83 Hz** / ACT-on-Orin-Nano 27.8 Hz / GR00T-on-Thor 10.9–24 Hz / SmolVLA 1.4 Hz / **frontier LLM 0.2–0.4 Hz**. Four bands, ~5 orders of magnitude, and the finding that **nothing closes the gap by inference speed** — hierarchy (S1/S2), action chunking, and async inference do. Reframes the 83 Hz figure: it names a band *nothing in this wiki deploys into*, so the comparison that matters is LLM vs the **VLA planner tier**, not vs the servo loop. (2026-07-27)
- [Robot platforms — comparison](syntheses/platforms/robot-platforms-comparison.md) — at-a-glance table of every robot entity in the wiki by tier / type / use; now includes [XLeRobot](entities/xlerobot.md) as the educational-tier bimanual mobile manipulator (vs Mobile ALOHA, ~25× apart); flags missing humanoids + cross-tier transfer gap. (2026-05-08)
- [Humanoid platforms survey](syntheses/platforms/humanoid-platforms-survey.md) — **also expanded 2026-08-28** with [Walden](entities/walden-robotics.md), the wheeled counterargument tier, and a **fifth axis: how the safety case gets solved** — inherit the AMR regime (Walden) or write a new standard (Figure). Companion to robot-platforms-comparison focused on humanoids; 10 entities listed by tier; AI-strategy archetypes + price stratification. **Expanded 2026-08-28** with a [Figure 03](entities/figure-03.md) deep-dive: the first closed-tier platform here to stop being a stub, three unsourced numbers the whole web repeats (168 cm / 40 DOF / 4 h), and a **fourth strategic axis — design-for-manufacture** — on which Figure's **1 robot/hour** at [BotQ](entities/botq.md) looks like the widest moat in the tier, and it isn't an AI moat. Also: "internal-only" no longer means "prototype." (2026-05-08, updated 2026-08-28)
- [Household robot decision — Stretch vs Unitree G1](syntheses/platforms/household-robot-decision-stretch-vs-g1.md) — buying-decision comparison for navigate + floor pickup + dishes + cans use case. Recommends Stretch. (2026-05-08)
- [Jetson Thor vs DGX Spark](syntheses/platforms/jetson-thor-vs-dgx-spark.md) — train-on-Spark / deploy-on-Thor split; RT-cores as the gating capability for Isaac Sim/Lab; decision tree for which-NVIDIA-box-for-what. (2026-05-16)
- [Onboard compute for XLeRobot — Orin Nano vs Orin NX vs AGX Orin vs AGX Thor](syntheses/platforms/jetson-onboard-compute-xlerobot.md) — which Jetson rides on an untethered XLeRobot. Power budget (288 Wh / 300 W) is decisive: **Orin Nano (7–25 W, $249) = validated default** (ACT @ 28 Hz; diffusion/SmolVLA ~1–2 Hz; 8 GB cap), **Orin NX 16 GB (10–40 W, ~$600, 157 TOPS) = drop-in VLA-upgrade sweet spot** (same Nano carrier), **AGX Orin (15–60 W, 64 GB) = max VLA-on-battery**, **Thor (40–130 W) = over-budget** for stock. Grounded by [Cutting the Cord](sources/cutting-the-cord-untethered-xlerobot.md). (2026-06-03)
- [GR00T inference on Jetson — Orin NX 16 GB vs AGX Orin 64 GB vs AGX Thor](syntheses/platforms/gr00t-inference-on-jetson.md) — measured [GR00T](entities/nvidia-groot.md)-3B rates per tier: **[Thor](entities/jetson-thor.md) 10.9 Hz official TensorRT / 22–24 Hz community**, **AGX Orin 5.8 Hz**, **Orin NX 16 GB unbenchmarked + below the 16 GB memory floor (~2–3 Hz extrapolated) → serve off-board via ZMQ**. Replan-vs-control-rate framing (chunked actions make ~11 Hz usable); all published numbers are N1.6 — no N1.7 latency exists yet. Answers the Thor entity page's open question. (2026-07-08)
- [Jetson module ladder — performance and power](syntheses/platforms/jetson-module-ladder-power-performance.md) — the **hardware-neutral superset reference**: all 8 shipping SKUs (Orin Nano 4 GB → Thor T5000) plus the announced T3000/T2000/IGX (GA Q1 2027) in one spec table; **both `nvpmodel` chapters merged** into a single mode ladder; **TOPS/W** (Orin NX 16 GB 3.9 / AGX Orin 64 GB 4.6 = family peak; **AGX Orin 32 GB is *less* efficient than Orin NX 16 GB**); the three structural Orin↔Thor mode differences (Super Mode locked at flash time; Thor degrades by GPU partition 10→6 TPC not clock; Thor's separate 168 W system cap); platform breaks (no RT cores, **no MIPI-CSI / 40-pin GPIO** on Thor); and an explicit gap list (no TOPS-per-mode or measured wall-power anywhere in the wiki). (2026-07-26)
- [Open-source robot AI research projects — landscape](syntheses/platforms/open-source-robot-ai-projects.md) — grouped catalog of every open-source project tracked in this wiki: LeRobot ecosystem, JEPA / world-model code, open VLAs, BC baselines, Karpathy's repos, whole-organism fly, open simulators, Farama RL stack, open robot platforms, the orgs behind them. (2026-05-17)
- [VLA deployability landscape — the four axes](syntheses/platforms/vla-deployability-landscape.md) — scores the wiki's VLAs on **openness / hardware cost / latency / fine-tuned success** ([MolmoAct2](entities/molmoact2.md)'s deployment framing). Corners: π-series = performant-but-closed-and-expensive; [SmolVLA](entities/smolvla.md) = open+affordable-but-smaller; GR00T/OpenVLA-OFT/Cosmos = open-weights-not-open-data; MolmoAct2 = the first *open* VLA claiming all four at once. Caveat: its 55.8 Hz is H100, not edge — on-Jetson latency unverified. (2026-07-25)
- [NVIDIA GPU rental landscape](syntheses/platforms/nvidia-gpu-rental-landscape.md) — providers, pricing, and how to choose: NVIDIA-native (Brev, DGX Cloud, Launchables), AI-focused clouds (RunPod, Lambda Labs, CoreWeave, Vast.ai, Modal), hyperscalers, DGX Spark-specific (Enverge $0.48/hr, Server Room, Primcast), peer-to-peer. H100 spans $1.25–$6.98/hr across 15+ providers in mid-2026. (2026-05-17)
- [Hailo NPU (AI HAT+ 2) vs Jetson (CUDA) for an onboard XLeRobot brain](syntheses/platforms/hailo-npu-vs-jetson-xlerobot.md) — the NPU-vs-CUDA decision: an NPU runs only HEF-**compiled** models, a Jetson runs PyTorch policies as-is. Map by *layer*: AI HAT+ 2 (Hailo-10H, $180) for onboard **perception + LLM/VLM agent**, Jetson Orin Nano/NX for the **control policy** (ACT/diffusion/SmolVLA). Usually **both**. (2026-06-07)
- [Anker SOLIX portable power — C300 DC vs C300 vs C1000](syntheses/platforms/anker-portable-power-stations.md) — buying-decision comparison for a DC mobile robot ([XLeRobot](entities/xlerobot.md) + [Thor](entities/jetson-thor.md)). Two different 288 Wh "C300"s — **both have a 12 V/10 A car outlet + USB-C** (DC Power Bank A1726, **2.8 kg**, hard 300 W cap; AC Power Station A1722, **4.1 kg, + 2× AC + 600 W surge**) — + the 1056 Wh **C1000 (12.9 kg)**. Verdict: **C300 DC = lightest/cheapest, paper-validated default**; **A1722 = +600 W-surge upgrade** (this build's pick); C1000 as bench/charging dock (too heavy to ride). (2026-06-03)

### Projects
- [Does latent-space inspection predict policy collapse?](syntheses/projects/latent-inspection-policy-collapse.md) — a **runnable experimental design**, not a result. [LIBERO-PRO](sources/libero-pro-paper.md) showed >90% → **0.0%** under perturbation and gave no way to know in advance; [Goodfire](entities/goodfire.md) sells exactly that capability with no published evidence. Five candidate predictors computed **without** the perturbed benchmark, the sharpest being an **episode-identity probe** (if you can linearly decode *which demonstration* a latent came from, the policy is indexing demonstrations rather than perceiving state). Names the design's central trap — using the *same* perturbations the benchmark applies means you've built a cheap benchmark, not a predictor — and escapes it via held-out perturbation families and perturbation-free metrics. Key move: go **longitudinal** (one policy, ~50 checkpoints) rather than cross-sectional (~10 open models), which fixes the power problem and tests whether memorization is visible in the representation *before* it is visible in behaviour. Open checkpoints, one GPU, no robot; plus a one-week [EchoJEPA](entities/echojepa.md) pilot with existing ground truth. **A negative result is publishable.** (2026-08-30)
- [XLeRobot — navigate, pick-and-place, teleoperate](syntheses/projects/xlerobot-nav-manip-teleop-bringup.md) — **sequenced bring-up plan** from an assembled, already-teleoperating robot; the concrete execution of [fleet-ladder](syntheses/projects/fleet-agentic-framework.md) steps 0–1. Key reframe: **navigation is the least risky leg** ([Cutting the Cord](sources/cutting-the-cord-untethered-xlerobot.md) validated RTAB-Map + [Nav2](entities/nav2.md) on a *weaker* board in this exact build), the hard parts are **demo quality** and **the one-owner-per-serial-port conflict** between ROS 2 and [LeRobot](entities/lerobot.md) on the arm bus. Policy path is **[ACT](entities/act.md) onboard first** (36 ms / 27.8 Hz — the only one of three that runs at control rate) then **[SmolVLA](entities/smolvla.md) served off-board from the [Spark](entities/dgx-spark.md)** (78.3% real-world on this exact arm). Carries the **top-down-grasp kinematic rule** from the 5-DoF analysis as a task-design constraint. (2026-08-13)
- [Can RoboTwin 2.0 generate data for a 5-DoF arm?](syntheses/projects/five-dof-arms-in-robotwin.md) — **experiment plan**, a week of work, everything MIT/Apache and released. Extends [RoboTwin 2.0](entities/robotwin.md)'s Piper result (2.4% → 25.1% with grasp adaptation; Franka −0.1) down one DoF to the [SO-ARM101](entities/so-arm101.md) / [XLeRobot](entities/xlerobot.md) / [Sourccey](entities/sourccey.md) tier. Includes a kinematic prediction that **RoboTwin's augmentation is pointed the wrong way for this arm** (5-DoF is fully dexterous *top-down* and constrained *laterally* — the opposite of Piper), the control that makes it an experiment (**a virtual 6-DoF twin of the same URDF**, isolating DoF from reach), and a hard blocker: **WSL cannot run it** (rendering ❌). (2026-08-13)
- [Pinball-playing robot — project scoping](syntheses/projects/pinball-playing-robot.md) — a mobile robot that navigates a showroom and learns to play pinball, on the [XLeRobot](entities/xlerobot.md) + [Jetson Thor](entities/jetson-thor.md) stack. Core reframe: **decompose by timescale** — slow/semantic ([GR00T](entities/nvidia-groot.md) ~10 Hz) vs. reflex flipper control (100+ Hz, separate loop). **Solenoid flipper effectors** parked by the [SO-101](entities/so-arm101.md) arms; cabinet-hugging rig; global-shutter/predict-don't-react reflex budget; score-delta reward; imitation-first → RL phase 3. (2026-07-15; tracker choice revised 2026-07-21)
- [The YOLO version lineage (v1 → v26)](syntheses/vision/yolo-version-lineage.md) — reconciles the three surveys + two primary papers into one annotated timeline. YOLO is **many groups**, not one roadmap; strip the names and every jump is one of **four moves** (anchors added-then-removed, multi-scale necks, free training tricks, NMS→NMS-free). The persistent ceiling — small objects in groups — is the un-benchmarked **STAL-vs-heatmaps** question this shares with the fast-ball thread. (2026-07-23)
- [Fast-ball tracking for robots — what transfers from broadcast sports CV](syntheses/projects/fast-ball-tracking-for-robots.md) — the [TrackNet](entities/tracknet.md) literature audited for **real-time robot use**. Perception core transfers (heatmaps beat YOLO by ~30 F1 at 2–12 px; motion attention is ~free) and **revises the pinball page's tracker choice**. But **V3's InpaintNet is non-causal** — its whole recall gain comes from a module a reflex loop can't run; that job belongs to the Kalman predictor. Static-camera assumption makes **pinball an near-ideal fit** (fixed rig + planar playfield) and **table tennis the harder target** (ego-motion + 3-D). Plus: no TrackNet-on-Jetson benchmark exists, and you must label your own data. **§8 Field evidence (2026-07-21)** tests every recommendation against the first-party [pinball_tracker](sources/pinball-tracker-repo.md) — heatmap-over-boxes and the causality discipline confirmed; **bootstrap labeling refuted** (8% of frames) and the **data-cost estimate revised ~20× down**. **§9 (2026-07-22, corrected)** then partly un-revises that: a third machine scores **0.555 F1** vs the second's 0.914 — a real per-venue penalty, though **not** the collapse first reported (a 5-frame label misalignment, caught by watching an overlay video, had made it read 0.232 and appear to reproduce V1's tennis→badminton failure; that claim is withdrawn). A fixed-volume control attributes **+0.043 to training diversity** and **+0.040 to sample count**, while augmentation is ~flat (+0.011). **2026-07-23:** two *clutter-matched* training machines lift the val-selected Pokémon score to **0.606** and precision to **0.735** (highest recorded) — diversity helps again, but labeling for the failure mode improved the error *profile* more than the headline F1, keeping **V3 background estimation** the argued next lever since the residual loss is still *static* clutter. **§9 (2026-07-24): background estimation built and confirmed** — a median-background aux input lifted the held-out cluttered machines **Pokémon 0.606→0.687, GotG 0.780→0.968**, precision-driven exactly as predicted; the first architectural lever that clearly worked, partly superseding the diversity/labeling levers. (2026-07-24)
- [Does a reComputer Robotics J4012 fit on a LeKiwi base?](syntheses/projects/j4012-on-lekiwi-base-fit.md) — mechanical-fit analysis. Measured LeKiwi base plate **~216 × 213 mm** (CAD) vs J4012 **130 × 121 × 66 mm**: **fits the footprint** and **clears the 4″ wheels** if top-deck-mounted without rim overhang, but **the arm owns the center** so it needs a **raised-tier/offset mount** clearing the shoulder swing — plus its own **19–54 V** rail. (2026-07-15)
- [Fleet agentic control framework — LeRobot + ROS 2 + on-edge Gemma + DGX Spark master control](syntheses/projects/fleet-agentic-framework.md) — full scope for **one framework across a heterogeneous fleet** ([XLeRobot](entities/xlerobot.md) + [LeKiwi](entities/lekiwi.md) on Orin NX 16 GB, [ROSOrin Pro](entities/rosorin-pro.md) on Orin Nano, [DGX Spark](entities/dgx-spark.md) hub). Three-layer architecture (ROS 2 + LeRobot policy via [Rosetta](entities/rosetta.md) / on-edge [Gemma 4](entities/gemma4.md) E4B agent / Spark master control); the **ROS 2↔MCP server** you build; central-MCP-then-[A2A](concepts/agents/llm-agent-architecture.md) coordination; HF data flywheel + HIL-SERL; ACT-onboard/SmolVLA-async; a 7-step de-risking ladder. (2026-07-04)
- [Fleet framework — implementation notes (MCP tool schema + scheduled training)](syntheses/projects/fleet-framework-implementation-notes.md) — code-level appendix to the fleet framework: the concrete **MCP tool schema** (JSON `tools/list` + structured return envelope + per-embodiment config) for the SO-ARM101 robots, and the **scheduled-training pipeline** on the [DGX Spark](entities/dgx-spark.md) (systemd timer/service, `train_fleet.sh` with a promotion gate, cross-embodiment co-train, HIL-SERL loop). (2026-07-04)
- [GR00T on DGX Spark over ZMQ to XLeRobot (Orin NX 16 GB) — performance estimate](syntheses/projects/gr00t-spark-zmq-xlerobot.md) — the fleet's off-board VLA serving leg, quantified: Spark inference **≈ Thor-class ~90–120 ms** (shared 273 GB/s bandwidth is the binding constraint; no published benchmark) + ZMQ hop (~10 ms wired, 15–30 ms Wi-Fi + jitter) → **~8–10 Hz wired / 6–9 Hz Wi-Fi replan**; chunked execution (8 actions ≈ 270 ms @ 30 Hz servos) keeps the arm fed; ~150–250 ms reactivity cost, RTC mitigates. Same replan rate as an on-robot Thor, zero robot watts. (2026-07-08)
- [ROS 2 ↔ MCP server — design doc](syntheses/projects/ros2-mcp-server-design.md) — design for the fleet's load-bearing new code: a server exposing a ROS 2 robot's skills as MCP tools (config-driven tool filtering, structured-result envelope, deterministic dispatch, out-of-band stop, stdio/SSE transport). Documents the [`ros2-mcp-server`](entities/ros2-mcp-server.md) skeleton repo (MIT, [on GitHub](sources/ros2-mcp-server-github.md)). (2026-07-04; updated 2026-07-05)
- [AgenticROS vs the fleet framework — does it change any decisions, and what to leverage](syntheses/projects/agenticros-vs-fleet-framework.md) — decision analysis of [AgenticROS](entities/agenticros.md) against the fleet framework + [ros2-mcp-server](entities/ros2-mcp-server.md). Verdict: validates the design (4/5 decisions independently converged), kills the "no community ros-mcp-server" claim, reshapes build-ladder steps 3/6 (copy its heartbeat + capability-routing + mission-graph patterns), but **does not replace ros2-mcp-server** — AgenticROS has no manipulation/LeRobot path. (2026-07-05)
- [LeWM on ROSOrin Pro — feasibility analysis](syntheses/projects/lewm-on-rosorin-pro-feasibility.md) — what's missing to deploy LeWM on Hiwonder ROSOrin Pro; realistic path; risks. (2026-05-08)
- [LeWM on Stretch — feasibility analysis](syntheses/projects/lewm-on-stretch-feasibility.md) — companion: Stretch resolves the teleop-data blocker via RUM's open dataset; concrete LeWM-vs-RUM-BC experiment design. (2026-05-08)
- [JEPA project ladder for ROSOrin Pro](syntheses/projects/jepa-project-ladder-rosorin-pro.md) — six-rung educational/research project ladder for learning JEPA on ROSOrin Pro hardware. (2026-05-08)
- [ROSOrin Pro — Lego pick-and-place project plan](syntheses/projects/rosorin-pro-lego-pick-place.md) — the BC-path sibling to the JEPA ladder; three tiers (OpenClaw color-threshold → LeRobot ACT/DP behavior cloning → GR00T fine-tune); recommends Tier 2 for "robust enough to use." (2026-05-15)
- [LeWM hello world — Project 1 detailed scope](syntheses/projects/lewm-hello-world-project-scope.md) — phase-by-phase plan for reproducing LeWM PushT, training from scratch, one-knob ablation. (2026-05-08)

- [Jetson Orin Nano — flash Jetson OS to NVMe SSD howto](syntheses/projects/jetson-orin-nano-flash-howto.md) — operational guide: SDK Manager or CLI `l4t_initrd_flash.sh`; QSPI bootloader caveat for pre-mid-2023 dev kits. (2026-05-16)
- [Wiki-query agent on DGX Spark — deployment plan](syntheses/projects/wiki-query-agent-on-dgx-spark.md) — scoping plan for serving this wiki as a queryable agent from a local DGX Spark running Qwen 2.5 72B Q8 via vLLM. Compares Anthropic-API / RAG-site / MCP / local-LLM paths; explains why Spark over Thor (RT cores for the dev-box use case); includes rent-before-buy section (Spark cloud rentals from Enverge at $0.48/hr). (2026-05-17)
- [LeRobot on ROSOrin Pro — adaptation plan for in-home floor-pickup-and-tidy](syntheses/projects/lerobot-on-rosorin-pro.md) — concrete porting plan (wrap `~/arm_group_control` ROS service vs direct STM32 serial; HX-12H ≠ FeeTech/Dynamixel SDK gap; Aurora930 12 fps vs LeRobot 30 Hz default) + 4-step ladder toward "navigate the house, pick objects off the floor, tidy them." Recommendation: keep OpenClaw as orchestrator, swap deterministic `/start_pick` for a LeRobot-trained ACT/SmolVLA policy fed by Dobb·E-style stick demos. (2026-05-28)
- [XLeRobot camera options for low-light + clutter](syntheses/projects/xlerobot-camera-options-low-light.md) — recommends swapping the stock RealSense D415 for the **D435i** (global shutter, wide FOV, IMU; needs a mount tweak — D435i 90×25×25 mm ≠ D415 99×20×23 mm, press-fit shell won't fit as-printed) for low-light/cluttered operation; explains active-IR-stereo gives depth-in-the-dark but RGB stays dark (LeRobot policies are RGB-driven → add illumination); D455 too big for the mount, D405 has no IR projector. (2026-05-30)
- [XLeRobot → D435i printable bracket (artifact pointer)](syntheses/projects/xlerobot-d435i-bracket.md) — in-vault stub for the parametric L-bracket in `hardware/xlerobot-d435i-bracket/` (STL + OpenSCAD); bolts to the D435i 45 mm M3 front pattern since the stock D415 shell won't fit. (2026-05-30)
- [XLeRobot + AGX Thor power budget — is a 300 W battery enough?](syntheses/projects/xlerobot-thor-power-budget.md) — the Anker C300's 300 W *rate* is fine (600 W surge covers transients); binding constraints are output-port wiring (12 V motors + 28 V/PD Thor, no single port does both) and **capacity** — adding a Thor drops runtime from 10+ hr to ~1.4–3.0 hr. Now assumes **software-capping Thor via `nvpmodel`** (70 W Mode 3): bounds peak draw, removes surge dependence, +15–25 % heavy-mode runtime, lets USB-C alone power the compute — at ~40 % GPU-throughput cost. Buy more Wh, not more W; prefer a DC-native pack. (2026-06-03)

### World models
- [What world models are measurably good for](syntheses/world-models/what-world-models-are-measurably-good-for.md) — four roles, four verdicts, from the [WorldArena](entities/worldarena.md) cluster. **Corrected 2026-08-08**: the JEPA family isn't unmeasured, it's measured by *incommensurable instruments* — and the first shared probes now exist. **RL environment: works** (~⅔ of the gap to simulator-RL). **Policy evaluator: ranks, doesn't measure.** **Data engine: marginal.** **Action planner: no** (3–4× worse than [π0.5](entities/pi-zero-5.md)). One line: *learned dynamics are good enough to shape a policy, not to be one.* Also: the text-vs-action-conditioned [Cosmos](entities/nvidia-cosmos.md) natural experiment, and the JEPA family being **entirely unmeasured** by these instruments. (2026-08-08)

### Agents
- [Action representation languages](syntheses/agents/action-representation-languages.md) — **is there a subset of natural language for robot actions?** A spectrum from free-form English → semantic subtask → RT-H language motions → PDDL/behavior trees → code+API → visual traces → action-as-text → **unified latent codebook tokens**, scored on readability vs embodiment-agnosticism. The finding: those two properties are **diverging**, not converging — 2026 cross-embodiment work bought portability *with* readability. Three wiki measurements bear on it: [CaP-X](sources/cap-x-paper.md)'s monotonic abstraction ladder (**the API designer supplies the performance**), [MolmoAct](sources/molmoact-paper.md)'s trace-editing beating language correction, and [TurboVLA](sources/turbovla-paper.md)'s task-ID-vs-English gap (95.4 vs 97.7) as the cost of a closed vocabulary. Ends with a two-layer design sketch and a one-training-run experiment. (2026-08-04)
- [Guardrails for robot agents — where the safety layer actually goes](syntheses/agents/guardrails-for-robot-agents.md) — the **five-layer safety cake** (physical interlocks → skill preconditions → execution rail → text rails → model alignment), built by four communities that don't cite each other. Three findings: an **MCP allowlist *is* an execution rail** (the [fleet's ros2-mcp-server](syntheses/projects/ros2-mcp-server-design.md) independently derived it — graded A–; Hiwonder's `eval()` graded F); **text rails are a base-URL swap** and nobody has taken them; and **the perception channel is wide open** — physical prompt injection ("leave a note where the robot will look"), unguarded in every stack, red-teamed by nobody. Ends with a build-ladder-mapped punch list. (2026-07-13)
- [Where the compute lives — agents on the robot vs on a local AI server](syntheses/agents/on-device-and-on-robot-agents.md) — **deployment-topology** analysis: three tiers (on-robot edge / local AI server on the LAN / cloud endpoint), the split-brain pattern (fast policy on-robot + reasoning offloaded), the model-fits-hardware ladder ([Gemma 4](entities/gemma4.md) E2B→31B; [Hermes](entities/hermes-agent.md) 8B→120B), and why privacy/latency/cost push reasoning onto a *local* server. Complements the framework + shape syntheses. (2026-07-04)
- [LLM-agent architecture across stacks](syntheses/agents/llm-agent-architecture-across-stacks.md) — now a **four-way** comparison: stretch_ai, ROSOrin, OpenClaw, and **[DimOS](entities/dimos.md)**. **Revised 2026-08-13** — the fourth stack, at 3,874★ and an order of magnitude larger than the others, **falsifies two of the four convergences** (small-Qwen default; hand-rolled JSON schemas → cloud LLM + **MCP**) and forces a narrower restatement of the page's headline finding: the VLA/agentic bifurcation now means **the two paths share a data format ([LeRobot](entities/lerobot.md) v3.0), not a control path**. Convergences 3 and 4 hold across all four. (2026-05-07, revised 2026-08-13)
- [Whole-organism agentic AI](syntheses/agents/whole-organism-agentic-ai.md) — brain (FlyWire connectome + LIF dynamics) + body (flybody MuJoCo) for *Drosophila*; first plausible end-to-end animal-scale agent; contrasts with robotics-flavoured agentic AI. (2026-05-08)
- [OpenClaw vs Hermes Agent as a robot's high-level thinker and planner](syntheses/agents/openclaw-vs-hermes-as-robot-brain.md) — 3-path framework (brain swap / agent-loop migration / full Hermes+ros-mcp-server+LeRobot rebuild) reframed as a two-layer choice (brain + agent loop), with Hiwonder's [`openclaw_controller`](entities/openclaw-controller.md) bridge as the robot-side mechanism either path inherits. Recommendation: start with Path A (local-LLM brain swap), add Path B selectively for Honcho memory + sub-agents + self-evolving skills. (2026-05-28)

### Sequence models
- [From n-grams to attention — the lineage that produced the Transformer](syntheses/sequence-models/language-model-to-transformer-lineage.md) — the seven-paper arc 2003 → 2017, all ingested as primaries. **Each paper removes one component of its predecessor**: word2vec deletes the hidden layer, negative sampling deletes the softmax, attention deletes the fixed-length bottleneck, the Transformer deletes the recurrence — and **the embedding table is never deleted**. Three lessons that transfer: the expensive op is always the one touching every element of a large discrete set; ideas arrive ~a decade before the compute (Bengio's §5.2 listed four of word2vec's contributions in 2003); and **winning the benchmark and winning the decade are different events** — seq2seq's own table reports attention losing 28.45 to 34.81. Closes with why this argues **for** inductive bias in robot learning: the architectures that discarded priors did so after their data grew four orders of magnitude, and robot data has not. (2026-08-30)

### RL
- [Humanoid whole-body control lineage — teleoperation first](syntheses/rl/humanoid-wbc-lineage.md) — companion to the quadruped lineage, and the contrast is the point: over 2024–25 the humanoid branch **kept and deepened privileged distillation** while the quadruped branch discarded it. Five papers as four cuts at *"the robot is not the human and the simulator is not the world"*: [H2O](sources/h2o-paper.md) filters infeasible **data**, [OmniH2O](sources/omnih2o-paper.md) unifies **interfaces**, [HOVER](sources/hover-paper.md) unifies **modes**, [ASAP](sources/asap-paper.md) corrects **dynamics**, [HumanPlus](sources/humanplus-paper.md) closes the whole loop to autonomy. Argues the divergence comes from having an *external reference signal* (retargeted human motion) worth distilling toward, a much larger action space, and HOVER's finding that distillation is an **improvement, not a compromise**. (2026-08-29)
- [Locomotion adaptation lineage — RMA to LocoFormer](syntheses/rl/locomotion-adaptation-lineage.md) — three papers, largely the same people, 2021→2025, each **deleting** the previous answer's machinery. [RMA](sources/rma-paper.md) (privileged physics → 8-dim extrinsics, 0.5 s window) → [egocentric vision](sources/egocentric-vision-locomotion-paper.md) (privileged geometry → depth, GRU memory, a proved distillation bound) → [LocoFormer](sources/locoformer-paper.md) (**no teacher at all**, ~18 s across trials, ten unseen bodies). The argument: **engineered structure was how you bought adaptivity when context was unaffordable** — RMA's whole apparatus exists to fit an [A1](entities/unitree-a1.md)'s onboard compute, and LocoFormer names resource intensity as its first limitation. What generalization is *over* moved with the budget: terrain → terrain-ahead → **bodies**. (2026-08-29)
- [Atari RL lineage — from ALE to Agent57 and MuZero](syntheses/rl/atari-rl-lineage.md) — hub for the Atari/DQN material; DQN → Rainbow → A3C/PPO → Go-Explore/Agent57 → MuZero/Dreamer; why robotics moved on but kept the toolbox. (2026-05-15)
- [Why online MBRL lost to imitation/VLAs for robots (2022–2026)](syntheses/rl/online-mbrl-vs-imitation-robot-learning.md) — [DayDreamer](sources/daydreamer-paper.md)'s 2022 "learn on real robots, no sim" template vs the offline imitation/VLA paradigm that won. Argues the binding constraint became **generalization-per-human-effort, not sample efficiency**; reward-vs-demonstration as interface; sim got cheap; the LLM-scaling prior favored offline. Coda: model-based ideas migrated to simulator/data-engine ([DIAMOND](sources/diamond-paper.md), [Cosmos](entities/nvidia-cosmos.md)) and RL-as-post-training ([π*0.6](entities/pistar06.md)). (2026-07-09)

### Artificial Life
- [Local rules, global complexity: learned vs. evolved vs. emergent self-organization](syntheses/alife/local-rules-global-complexity.md) — cross-cutting analysis of the whole ALife branch. The shared bet (complex global behavior from simple local rules) read as a **"where does the local rule come from?" spectrum**: hand-designed (boids/Core War) → learned (NCA) → evolved (Tierra/Avida) → emergent (Computational Life), reframed as **"what information becomes structure?"** (designer → target-via-gradient → environment-via-selection → nothing). Mordvintsev as the learned↔emergent bridge; Xenobots as the all-three hybrid. Now also carries Wolfram's orthogonal **construction-vs-search** axis (where the *patterns* come from, given a fixed rule). (2026-06-02)

### Society
- [World-model policy claims vs. this wiki's technical evidence](syntheses/society/world-model-policy-vs-wiki-evidence.md) — scoring the [HAI brief](sources/hai-world-model-spatial-intelligence-brief.md) against ~37 world-model sources. **Right about what it's least equipped to know, thin on what it should have found easily.** Corroborated: the evaluation patchwork (and the wiki's version is worse — ~1,030 rollouts needed vs ~70 run), the per-architecture failure split, the taxonomy collapse. Complicated: "planners are least mature" is right *because the counter-evidence is unreliable*; the shared-action-data market failure sits next to [Open X-Embodiment](entities/open-x-embodiment.md)/[DROID](entities/droid.md); the cost claim has no numbers anywhere. (2026-08-07)
- [Three critiques of the LLM-as-intelligence North Star](syntheses/society/critiques-of-the-intelligence-north-star.md) — [LeCun](sources/lecun2022-path-towards-ami.md) (world models) vs [Jordan](sources/jordan-collectivist-economic-ai.md) (markets) vs [Constitution](sources/claudes-constitution.md) (alignment): three senior arguments that "scale the LLM toward intelligence" is the wrong target, and where they conflict (esp. alignment as **bright line vs. tradeoff**). (2026-05-29)
- [The Robot AI industry — structure, capital, and the gap between the two](syntheses/society/robot-ai-industry-map.md) — industry map: the five layers (silicon → actuators → platforms → models → deployment) and who is structurally safe at each; four postures (vertically integrated / model-layer / commodity hardware / research-subsidiary); **automotive companies that own or invest in robotics AI**, separating equity from purchase order — [Hyundai](entities/boston-dynamics.md) took Boston Dynamics to **wholly owned** in July 2026, [Toyota](entities/tri.md) owns TRI and anchor-funded [Walden](entities/walden-robotics.md), Mercedes-Benz is an Apptronik **investor and customer**, BMW is a Figure **customer only**; funding table; five checks for reading industry claims. Prices everything against the **12.4% [BEHAVIOR-1K](entities/behavior-benchmark.md)** number and Hyundai's own **2028 sequencing / 2030 assembly** guidance. Marks live-web facts as distinct from ingested sources. (2026-08-29)
- [Who benefits from consumer robotics in 2026–2027 (other than NVIDIA)](syntheses/society/consumer-robotics-value-chain.md) — value-chain reading of the consumer-robot wave from the BOMs and business models the wiki already holds. **Best evidenced: actuators** — [UME](entities/ume.md)'s BOM is **86% actuators**, and DoF count *is* the order size, so [FeeTech](entities/feetech.md) (~3× cheaper than [Dynamixel](entities/dynamixel.md), 50%+ of community LeRobotDatasets) is paid on the order clock regardless of whether products retain users. **Highest leverage, thinnest evidence: whoever serves the model** — three independent signs the consumer price point can't carry the compute ([M1](entities/zeroth-m1.md) publishes no compute spec and reportedly runs Gemini; [Vulcan](entities/vulcan-robotics.md) sells "rented compute"; [Sourccey](entities/sourccey.md) ships a 0.9 B policy beside a Pi 5 that can't run it), which would make the robot a subscription-acquisition device. Pushes back on the easy answers: **LeRobot has mindshare, not a documented monetization path**, and a $2,499 device makes $29,950 [Stretch](entities/stretch.md) look overpriced rather than validated. Closes on the split that matters — component suppliers are paid on **orders** (happening now), service layers on **retention** (requires shipments), against a documented **89.4% sim vs 12.4% [BEHAVIOR-1K](entities/behavior-benchmark.md)** capability gap. Carries an explicit epistemic warning: the wiki holds **no market data**. (2026-08-17)
